Before, the exchange failed at once and the next requests were not sent. If records are still not written, the exchange fails after the last request, with a RuntimeCamelException (a RuntimeException, as before) whose message gives the number of records written and not written. The CamelAwsKinesisRecordCount and CamelAwsKinesisFailedRecordCount headers are now also set when the exchange fails, including when a PutRecords call throws: the exchange then fails with the exception of the call, as before, and the records of that call and of the requests that were not sent are counted as not written. Because of the retries, a batch whose records all fail now blocks the caller longer before it fails (700 to 875 milliseconds per request, 14 to 17.5 seconds for 10,000 records), and a record that is sent again is written after the next records of its request. A redelivery of the exchange still sends the whole batch again: see the Batch Producer section of AWS Kinesis.

camel-azure-storage-blob - copyBlob validates the source account and container name

copyBlob now rejects invalid source account or container names (headers CamelAzureStorageBlobSourceBlobAccountName and CamelAzureStorageBlobSourceBlobContainerName) with an IllegalArgumentException.

camel-stitch - a response with an empty body, and the default sequence of a message

A response of Stitch with an empty body now sets the CamelStitchCode, CamelStitchStatus and CamelStitchHeaders headers, and the body is an empty message. Before, the exchange kept the request body and got none of these headers. An error response (HTTP status 300 or above) with an empty body now fails the exchange with a StitchException, as any other error response does. Before, it was ignored.

A StitchMessage without a sequence now gets the time it is built (in millis) as its sequence. Before, every message got the same sequence: the time the StitchMessage class was loaded.

camel-stripe - the CamelStripeOperation header chooses the operation

The CamelStripeOperation header now overrides the operation of the endpoint, as documented. Before, the header was ignored and the operation of the endpoint was always used. Remove the header from a message that should use the operation of the endpoint.

camel-telemetry - the span of an asynchronous step ends when the step is done

This applies to camel-opentelemetry2, camel-micrometer-observability and camel-telemetry-dev. The span of a step that calls back from another thread (such as delay, which is asynchronous by default, threads, throttle with asyncDelayed, or a bean that returns a CompletableFuture) now ends when the step is done, and the steps after it are children of the span that contains the step (the route span, or the span of an EIP such as choice). Since 4.17 (since 4.21 with the default options, which trace the core processors) the following steps were children of the span of the asynchronous step, and that span lasted until the end of the route. Around a called route the spans were ended by the wrong events, and with a recipientList or enrich to such a route some spans were never ended. In particular:

  • The trace tree of such routes changes, and the span of the asynchronous step is shorter.

  • A failure in a later step no longer marks the span of the asynchronous step as failed: the error is on the span of the failing step and on the spans that contain it.

  • Steps of an onException that run after the asynchronous step, and custom spans created in a processor after it, are no longer nested under its span.

  • An asynchronous step that fails and is then handled by an onException with handled(true) now has its span marked as an error, because the span ends before the error handler runs. A synchronous step already behaved that way.

This is the tree of Camel 4.16 and older, so there is no option to keep the 4.17 tree. A dashboard that read the time of the rest of the route from the span of the asynchronous step can use the route span instead.

camel-telemetry, camel-tracing - the http.url, url.path and server.address span attributes are presented like camel.uri

This applies to camel-opentelemetry2, camel-micrometer-observability, camel-telemetry-dev, camel-opentelemetry and camel-observation. The http.url, url.path and server.address attributes of a span are now presented the same way as the camel.uri attribute, which shows the endpoint URI as Endpoint.toString() does:

  • http.url, when the message has neither a CamelHttpUrl nor a CamelHttpUri header, is now the HTTP address the endpoint sends to, with the query parameters it sends but without the Camel endpoint options. Before, it was the endpoint URI from the last http: onwards, with all the endpoint options. A camel-http endpoint with an https address now sets the attribute as well, and for a camel-vertx-http endpoint with an https address the value no longer starts with http://https://.

  • url.path no longer includes the user info in front of the host of the endpoint URI.

  • server.address, and http.url taken from the CamelHttpUrl or CamelHttpUri header, are presented as camel.uri is.

Dashboards or queries that use the old values may need a change.

camel-oaipmh - the CamelOaimphOnlyFirst and CamelOaimphIgnoreSSLWarnings headers apply to their message only

The CamelOaimphOnlyFirst and CamelOaimphIgnoreSSLWarnings headers now apply only to the message that has them. Before, the producer wrote them onto the endpoint, so after one message with CamelOaimphOnlyFirst=true every later message sent to that endpoint returned only the first page, and after one message with CamelOaimphIgnoreSSLWarnings=true every later message accepted a self-signed certificate. A message without the header uses the onlyFirst and ignoreSSLWarnings options of the endpoint.

camel-clickhouse - a query in a binary format returns a byte[] body

A query with a binary format (such as RowBinary, Native, Parquet, Arrow or Avro) now returns the result as a byte[] body. Before, the bytes were decoded as UTF-8 text into a String, which replaced the bytes that are not valid UTF-8, so the result could not be read. A query with a text format (such as JSONEachRow, CSV or TSV) still returns a String.

Inserting a List of Map now fails with a ClickHouseException that says why, and so does a List of objects when no class is registered on the Client for the table. Before, both failed with a NullPointerException from the ClickHouse client.

camel-clickup - an event of another kind than taskTimeTrackedUpdated reaches the route

A webhook event of another kind than taskTimeTrackedUpdated, such as taskCreated, now reaches the route as a GenericEvent (getEvent() gives the kind, getProperties() the other fields of the JSON). Before, it failed before the route with 500, and ClickUp kept sending it again. A route that expects only TaskTimeTrackedUpdatedEvent can check the type of the body, as the example in the ClickUp component page does.

camel-cm-sms - the from of an SMSMessage is the sender of the SMS

An SMSMessage with a from is now sent with that sender. Before, the sender was set only when from was empty (to defaultFrom), so a message with its own from was sent with an empty sender. A message without a from is still sent with defaultFrom.

When the validation of an SMSMessage fails, the InvalidPayloadRuntimeException now names the invalid fields in its message, and its cause is an org.apache.camel.ValidationException.

camel-coap - a body that does not convert to a byte array fails

The coap producer sent a POST or PUT body that has no conversion to byte[], such as a Map, a POJO or a number, as an empty payload, without an error, so the data was lost. It now fails the exchange with an InvalidPayloadException. A message without a body is still sent with an empty payload.

The coap consumer answered such a reply body with 2.05 Content and an empty payload. It now answers 5.00 Internal Server Error, as the CoAP component page documents. A route without a reply body still answers 2.05 Content with an empty payload.

Marshal such a body first (for example to JSON) or convert it to a String.

camel-cometd - stream, file and null bodies are published

The cometd producer, and the reply of an InOut cometd consumer, now publish a stream, a Reader or a file body (File, Path or the body of the file consumer) as its content as text. Before, the clients received the text of its toString, such as ByteArrayInputStreamCache@…​ or the path of the file. A cometd consumer in the same JVM now receives such a body as a String.

A null body is now published as a JSON null that the subscribed clients receive. Before, the message had no data, so the clients took it for the reply of a publish and never received it, while the exchange succeeded.

camel-dapr - the producer keeps the message headers, and sends text and stream bodies as bytes

The dapr producer replaced all the headers of the message with the headers of the Dapr response, so every header set before the call was gone afterwards. It now keeps the headers of the message and adds the headers of the response. A CamelDapr* header set before a dapr call therefore also applies to the next dapr call of the route; remove it when the next call must use the options of its endpoint.

The invokeService, pubSub, invokeBinding and state save operations now convert a text body (String) and a stream body (InputStream, Reader) to bytes, which the Dapr client sends as they are. Before, the Dapr client serialized a text body as a JSON string, so hello was sent as "hello" with the quotes, and a JSON text was sent as a quoted, escaped JSON string; a stream body failed. A route that relied on the quoted text must now send the JSON it wants, for example with marshal().json(). Other bodies, such as a Map or a POJO, are still serialized as JSON. The workflow operations give the body to the workflow client as before.

camel-dapr - the secret store and key headers need the allowSecretStoreAndKeyFromHeaders option

The secret operation of the dapr producer took the secret store, the key and the metadata from the CamelDaprSecretStore, CamelDaprKey and CamelDaprMetadata headers when they were set, over the secretStore and key options of the endpoint, so a message could read another secret, or use another secret store, than the endpoint is configured with. The secret operation now takes the secret store, the key and the metadata from the endpoint, unless the endpoint sets the new allowSecretStoreAndKeyFromHeaders=true option (disabled by default), or the component sets camel.component.dapr.allowSecretStoreAndKeyFromHeaders=true. A route that relies on these headers must set this option.

The metadata of the endpoint is set with the new metadata. options, such as metadata.namespace=production. A secret route that sent the metadata in the CamelDaprMetadata header can move it to these options. The other operations also send the metadata of these options, and the CamelDaprMetadata header still takes their place for one message.

A secret endpoint without a key, which took the key from the CamelDaprKey header, now fails with an error that names the option, instead of reading all the secrets of the store. A secret endpoint without a secretStore, which took it from the CamelDaprSecretStore header, now fails because the secret store is missing.

The other operations do not change: the state operations still take the key from the CamelDaprKey header, the other operations still take the metadata from the CamelDaprMetadata header, and the other CamelDapr* headers still take the place of the options of the endpoint, as before.

camel-consul - no Optional in the body, and the CamelConsulResult header of every action

The GET_SESSIONS action of consul:kv, the RENEW action of consul:session and the GET action of consul:preparedQuery set the java.util.Optional of the Consul client as the body, and the CamelConsulResult header was always true. The body is now the value itself: the session id as a String, the SessionInfo or the StoredQuery. When there is none, the body is unchanged and CamelConsulResult is false, as with the INFO action of consul:session.

The actions of consul:agent and consul:status now set the CamelConsulResult header, as the other actions do. REGISTER and DEREGISTER of consul:catalog now set it to true instead of false.

The FIRE action of consul:event with a message without body failed with a NullPointerException. It now fires the event without payload.

camel-aws - the HTTP client is no longer looked up on the classpath

The camel-aws components now set the HTTP client of the AWS SDK clients they create for their endpoints: the Apache 5 client (software.amazon.awssdk:apache5-client) for synchronous clients, and the Netty client (software.amazon.awssdk:netty-nio-client) for asynchronous clients. These are the clients the AWS SDK selects itself with the Camel dependencies. Before, unless a proxy or trustAllCertificates was configured, the AWS SDK looked the HTTP client up on the classpath. That lookup fails with java.util.ServiceConfigurationError (…​ not a subtype) when the classpath registers an HTTP client from another copy of the AWS SDK, as on a Kafka Connect worker.

If you replaced these dependencies to use another HTTP client implementation, name it with the AWS SDK system property software.amazon.awssdk.http.service.impl (synchronous clients) or software.amazon.awssdk.http.async.service.impl (asynchronous clients): Camel then leaves the choice of the HTTP client to the AWS SDK.

camel-aws-bedrock - retrieval pagination

The retrieve operation of aws-bedrock-agent-runtime now removes the CamelAwsBedrockAgentRuntimeNextToken header when the response has no continuation token. Previously, an incoming token could remain on the final page. Pagination loops should stop when this header is absent. The CamelAwsBedrockAgentRuntimeRetrieveGuardrailAction header is also removed when the response has no guardrail action, so an action from an earlier page cannot survive.

camel-aws-bedrock - the invoke operations send the body, not its toString()

The invoke operations of aws-bedrock now convert the message body to bytes, so a byte[], an InputStream or a file is sent as its content, and a Map or a Jackson JsonNode is marshalled to JSON. Before, the body was sent as its toString(), so only a String body worked. A message with no body now fails with an InvalidPayloadException, where before the text null was sent to the model.

Other changes of aws-bedrock:

  • invokeTextModel with amazon.titan-embed-text-v2:0 now fails with an IllegalArgumentException that points to invokeEmbeddingsModel, as with the other embedding models. Before, it gave the JSON of the embedding as text.

  • The streaming operations fail with an IllegalArgumentException for an image generation or embedding model, which do not stream. Before, invokeImageModelStreaming and invokeEmbeddingsModelStreaming gave an empty text.

  • With no operation option and no CamelAwsBedrockOperation header, the producer fails with an IllegalArgumentException that says so, instead of a NullPointerException.

  • The CamelAwsBedrockGuardrailTrace header is a GuardrailTraceAssessment, as converse always set it, and converseStream now sets it too. ConverseStreamHandler.StreamMetadata keeps it as a GuardrailTraceAssessment instead of a GuardrailTrace.

  • converseStream with guardrailTrace=true now asks for the guardrail trace.

  • invokeTextModel with an AI21 Jamba model gives the choices of the response, which was null before.

In the same way, aws-bedrock-agent-runtime now sends a Map or a Jackson JsonNode body as JSON text for retrieveAndGenerate, retrieve, invokeFlow, invokeAgent and invokeInlineAgent. Before, a Map was sent as its toString(), such as {question=What is Camel?}, and a JsonNode as pretty-printed JSON.

camel-aws-bedrock - invokeImageModelStreaming and invokeEmbeddingsModelStreaming are deprecated

The invokeImageModelStreaming and invokeEmbeddingsModelStreaming operations of aws-bedrock are deprecated, and will be removed in a later release. The image generation and embedding models do not stream their response, so these operations fail for such a model, and with a text model they are the same as invokeTextModelStreaming. Use invokeImageModel, invokeEmbeddingsModel or invokeTextModelStreaming instead.

camel-aws2-sts - the secret access key and the session token are no longer copied into headers

The assumeRole, getSessionToken and getFederationToken operations of aws2-sts no longer set the CamelAwsStsSecretKey and CamelAwsStsSessionToken headers. The body is the response of the AWS SDK, such as an AssumeRoleResponse, and its credentials() hold them: use ${body.credentials().secretAccessKey()} and ${body.credentials().sessionToken()} instead of the headers. The CamelAwsStsAccessKeyId, CamelAwsStsExpiration and CamelAwsStsAssumedRoleArn headers are still set.

The STS2Constants.SECRET_KEY_ID and STS2Constants.SESSION_TOKEN constants are deprecated.

camel-weather - the CamelWeatherLocation header is the location that is queried

The latitude and longitude of the current geolocation (a location of current, or no location) are no longer written onto the configuration of the endpoint. Before, they were, so every later query on that endpoint, including one with a CamelWeatherLocation header such as Paris, requested the weather of the current geolocation. An endpoint without a location already did the geolocation when its producer was created, so its CamelWeatherLocation header was never used. The lat and lon options of an endpoint are still used instead of the location.

camel-geocoder - Nominatim sets ZERO_RESULTS when it finds no place

With type=NOMINATIM, an address that Nominatim does not find (it answers []) now sets the CamelGeoCoderStatus header to ZERO_RESULTS. Before, the exchange failed with an IllegalArgumentException. A latitude and longitude with no place (Nominatim answers {"error":"Unable to geocode"}) now sets ZERO_RESULTS too, and another error answer of Nominatim sets ERROR. Before, both set OK with no other headers.

camel-xchange - an endpoint with a method of another service fails

An xchange endpoint now fails when it is created if it has no service or no method, or if the method belongs to another service, such as service=marketdata&method=currencies. Before, its producer did nothing and left the message unchanged. The Currency and CurrencyPair headers and the body can now be text such as BTC and BTC/USDT; text with a currency that XChange does not know fails the exchange.

camel-gson, camel-jsonb - the unmarshal type header needs allowUnmarshallType

The Gson and JSON-B data formats now use the CamelGsonUnmarshalType and CamelJsonbUnmarshallType headers to choose the class to unmarshal to only when the new allowUnmarshallType option is enabled, as the Jackson data formats do with CamelJacksonUnmarshalType. By default the headers are ignored, and the configured unmarshalType is used (a Map for a JSON object when none is configured). A route that sets the header must enable the option, for example with .unmarshal(new JsonDataFormat(JsonLibrary.Gson).allowUnmarshallType(true)) or <json library="Gson" allowUnmarshallType="true"/>.

camel-google-calendar-stream

With syncFlow=false and considerLastUpdate=true, the consumer now follows all result pages in each poll. Previously it only read the first page, which could prevent later events from ever being delivered. In this mode, maxResults controls the API page size; a poll can now deliver more events than this value. Non-sync polling with considerLastUpdate=false continues to read only one page per poll.

camel-google-mail, camel-google-drive, camel-google-calendar, camel-google-sheets - one API client per endpoint

The google-mail, google-mail-stream, google-drive, google-calendar, google-calendar-stream, google-sheets and google-sheets-stream components now create one API client per endpoint, from the options of that endpoint (clientId, clientSecret, refreshToken, accessToken, serviceAccountKey, delegate, scopes and applicationName, plus emailAddress, p12FileName and user on the two calendar components), instead of a single client per component that every endpoint of the component shared. Each endpoint keeps its client for its lifetime. The clientFactory option of a google-drive endpoint is now used to create the client of that endpoint. In addition, a google-mail-stream endpoint configured with neither clientSecret nor serviceAccountKey now fails with an IllegalArgumentException when its client is created, instead of getting a null client and failing at the first API call. The google-mail:update-message-labels data type transformer now uses a client created from the component-level configuration (the camel.component.google-mail.* options), kept until the component stops. Before, it used whichever client the component had already created, for example the one of a google-mail:messages/modify endpoint, so credentials set only on an endpoint URI no longer reach the transformer: set them on the component as well.

camel-grpc - the consumer reply must be the response message of the method

The grpc consumer sent the message body at the end of the route to the client as is. A body of another protobuf message type, such as the unchanged request, was serialized and decoded by the client as the response, with wrong field values and no error. The consumer now converts the reply (each element of a List reply) to the response message type of the called method, and when that is not possible it sends nothing and ends the call with an INTERNAL status error. A route must set the response message of the method as the body. A reply without a body still sends an empty response message.

The same applies to the replies that a producer with toRouteControlledStreamObserver=true sends for a consumer with routeControlledStreamObserver=true: a reply that does not convert ends the call with an INTERNAL status error and fails the exchange. Such a producer also sends each element of a List body as a message, where a List failed before. A CamelGrpcEventType header with a value other than onNext, onCompleted or onError now fails the exchange, where it sent nothing before. An onError event with a body that is not a Throwable now ends the call with an INTERNAL status error and fails the exchange with an IllegalArgumentException, where it failed with a ClassCastException or a NullPointerException and the client waited until its deadline.

camel-grpc - routeControlledStreamObserver applies to streaming requests with the PROPAGATION strategy

With routeControlledStreamObserver=true and the default PROPAGATION consumer strategy, the route now sends the replies of client streaming and bidirectional streaming calls, as it already did for unary and server streaming calls. The consumer passes the grpcResponseObserver exchange property to the route, and no longer sends the body at the end of the route or ends the call itself. Before, a producer with toRouteControlledStreamObserver=true failed with an IllegalArgumentException, and the consumer sent the body at the end of the route as the reply. Such a route must now send its replies with a producer with toRouteControlledStreamObserver=true and end the call, for example on the event forwarded with forwardOnCompleted=true. The events of a call are still routed one at a time, in order, which is the difference with the DELEGATION strategy.

camel-hashicorp-vault - cloud without a namespace, the createSecret body, and the secret version

With cloud=true and no namespace, the hashicorp-vault producer now works on the secrets engine itself, as the secret refresh task already did. Before, getSecret failed with Path must not be empty and listSecrets returned an empty list. A hashicorp: property with camel.vault.hashicorp.cloud=true and no namespace now also reads the secret from the engine itself. With cloud=true and a namespace, createSecret and deleteSecret now work on the secret in the namespace (<namespace>/<secretsEngine>), as getSecret and listSecrets do. Before, they worked on the engine outside the namespace.

createSecret now writes a body of text, bytes, a stream or a file that holds a JSON object as the fields of the secret; before, such a body failed. A body that is not a JSON object, and a message without a body, now fail the exchange. Before, a message without a body wrote a new, empty version of the secret.

The CamelHashicorpVaultSecretVersion header of getSecret must be a version number; any other value now fails the exchange with an IllegalArgumentException.

camel-hashicorp-vault - the token is renewed, and requests to Vault time out

The hashicorp-vault endpoint, the hashicorp: properties function and the secret refresh task now renew their token before it expires. With a token, Camel now looks the token up in Vault (auth/token/lookup-self) at its first request, and renews it (auth/token/renew-self) when the token is renewable and has a time to live. Vault’s default policy allows both. A token that cannot look itself up logs a warning, and is used as before, without renewal. Camel never revokes a token it is given; a token it gets from one of the new logins, chosen with the authentication option, is revoked when the endpoint, the function or the task stops.

Requests to Vault now time out after 5 seconds to connect, and after 15 seconds to read the response, which are the defaults of Spring Vault. Before, a request could wait forever.

The hashicorp: properties function now uses the VaultTemplate it is created with. Before, it replaced it with a client of the camel.vault.hashicorp configuration, and failed to start when that configuration had no token. A hashicorp-vault endpoint without a vaultTemplate now fails when it is created if its host, port or scheme is missing, or a setting of its login is missing. Before, a missing host failed only at the first request to Vault. The camel.vault.hashicorp.token option is no longer marked as required, as only the token login uses it.

The hashicorp-secrets dev console now shows the login (Token, AppRole, Kubernetes or JWT) instead of OAuth Token.

camel-hivemq - a body that does not convert to a byte array fails the exchange

The hivemq producer published a message body that has no conversion to byte[], such as a Map, a POJO or a number, as an empty payload, without an error, so the data was lost. It now fails the exchange with an InvalidPayloadException. Marshal such a body first (for example to JSON) or convert it to a String. A message without a body is still published as an empty payload.

camel-jcache - a JCacheManager closes only what no other manager uses

JCacheManager.close(), which a jcache endpoint calls when it stops, closed its cache, its CacheManager and the CachingProvider, which is shared by the whole JVM, and so the caches of every other endpoint, repository and CamelContext of that provider. It now closes each of them only when no other JCacheManager uses it. A JCacheManager that was closed no longer opens its cache: getCache() then throws IllegalStateException; before, it returned the closed cache, whose operations throw IllegalStateException. This matters for code that calls a JCacheManager (for example one created by a custom JCacheManagerFactory) after it was closed.

camel-jdbc, camel-spring-jdbc - plain ? placeholders take their values only from the CamelJdbcParameters header

With useHeadersAsParameters=true and a query with only plain ? placeholders (no named parameters :?name), the jdbc and spring-jdbc producers bound the values of all the message headers by position, or of the CamelJdbcParameters header when it was set. Which value went to which ? then depended on the headers of the message and their order (which changed in 4.21 from alphabetical to the order they were added). The values of plain ? placeholders now come only from the CamelJdbcParameters header: a List with the values in the order of the placeholders (or a Map, whose values are taken in the order of its entries, or a single value for a query with one ?, such as a String or a byte[]). When the header is not set, or has more or fewer values than the query has ? placeholders, the exchange fails with a RuntimeExchangeException (before, extra values were ignored). Set the values in that header, or use named parameters.

Named parameters take their values from the CamelJdbcParameters header when it is a Map, or else from the message headers, as before. A CamelJdbcParameters header that is not a Map (such as the List left by an earlier query with plain ? placeholders in the same route) is now ignored for named parameters, where before it made the exchange fail with Cannot find key.

camel-jgroups-raft - a body that does not convert to a byte array fails the exchange

The jgroups-raft producer skipped a message body that has no conversion to byte[], such as a Map, a POJO or a number, without an error, so nothing was set in the replicated log and the exchange succeeded. It now fails the exchange with an InvalidPayloadException. Marshal such a body first (for example to JSON) or convert it to a String. A message without a body is still skipped.

camel-keycloak - a failed create or delete fails the exchange, and a pojoRequest body is converted

The producer operations that give the JAX-RS Response of Keycloak as the body, such as createUser, deleteUser, createGroup, createClient and createOrganization, now fail the exchange with a jakarta.ws.rs.WebApplicationException when Keycloak answers with a status other than 2xx, such as 409 (already exists) or 404 (not found). Before, the exchange completed and the route had to check the status of the Response. deleteOrganization, removeOrganizationMember and unlinkOrganizationIdentityProvider fail the same way. The Response body is now closed: its status and headers, such as Location, can still be read, but not its entity. The bulk operations count such a status as a failed item.

With pojoRequest=true, a body that is not the Keycloak representation of the operation is converted: a Map or JSON text is mapped to it. A body that does not convert fails with an InvalidPayloadException; before, the operation did nothing and the body was kept. The elements of the List of bulkCreateUsers and bulkUpdateUsers are converted the same way; an element that does not convert fails the exchange before any call to Keycloak.

camel-keycloak - JWKS public key resolution rate limiting

KeycloakSecurityPolicy now rate limits the refresh of the public keys from the JWKS endpoint of Keycloak (/protocol/openid-connect/certs), so that a burst of tokens with unknown kid headers, or a key rotation, no longer contacts the endpoint once per request.

The new option minJwksRefreshInterval is the minimum interval in seconds between two refresh attempts, 10 by default. A token with an unknown kid triggers a refresh only when no attempt was made within that interval. Otherwise the token is rejected, because its key is not in the cache, and the endpoint is contacted again only once the interval has elapsed. A failed attempt counts as an attempt.

The cached keys are refreshed on a request once they are older than jwksRefreshInterval, in seconds and 300 by default. There is no background refresh.

camel-splunk-hec - bytes, stream and file bodies are sent as text

The producer now sends a byte array, an InputStream, a Reader or a file body (File, Path or the file of the file consumer) as text in the event. Before, a byte array was sent as a base64 string, a File as its path, and a stream failed after part of the request had already been sent.

The JSON of the event is now written before the request is sent, with a Content-Length instead of a chunked request body. A body or a header that cannot be written as JSON fails the exchange without sending anything to Splunk.

camel-spring-ai-embeddings - a List body always gives a List of vectors, and a Map or POJO body fails

A List body with one text now gives a List<float[]> with one vector, as a List of more texts does, and sets the CamelSpringAiEmbeddings and CamelSpringAiEmbeddingInputTexts headers. Before, it gave a single float[] and set the CamelSpringAiEmbedding, CamelSpringAiEmbeddingIndex and CamelSpringAiEmbeddingInputText headers, as for a String body. A String body still gives a single float[].

Each element of a List body is now read as text, and a null element fails with an IllegalArgumentException. Before, a List of other objects failed with a ClassCastException in the embedding model. An empty List now gives an empty List without calling the embedding model.

The body, and each element of a List body, must now be text, bytes, a stream, a Reader, a file or a Spring AI Document (its text is embedded). Any other value, such as a Map, a POJO or a number, fails the exchange with an InvalidPayloadException. Before, such a body was embedded as its toString(), for example {a=1, b=2}, and a Document too. Convert the body to text before the endpoint, with convertBodyTo(String.class) or a marshal to JSON.

This also applies to bodies that Camel can convert to text but that are not in the list above, and that were embedded as their text before: a char[], a Netty ByteBuf (for example from netty-http with disableStreamCache=true), and a custom type with its own type converter to String. A List with such an element fails too, as do a Set and an array of texts (only a java.util.List is embedded as a batch). Add convertBodyTo(String.class) before the endpoint for such a body, or convert each element of the List to a String.

camel-spring-ai-vector-store - DELETE takes the ids from the body first, and the embeddings headers go with a vector body

The DELETE operation now takes the ids from the body: an id, a Document, or a List of ids or of Document. The CamelSpringAiVectorStoreDocumentIds header is used only when the message has no body. Before, the header won over the body, and as ADD and SIMILARITY_SEARCH set this header to their own output ids, a later DELETE in the same route deleted those documents instead of the ids in the body. A route that sets the header and sends a body that is not the ids should remove the body (for example setBody(constant(null))) or put the ids in the body. Deleting what SIMILARITY_SEARCH found still works, as its body is the List of Document found. A List with an element that is not a String or a Document now fails.

The ADD operation now uses the CamelSpringAiEmbeddingInputText and CamelSpringAiEmbeddingInputTexts headers of spring-ai-embeddings only with a float[] or a List of float[] body. Before, a header left over from an earlier spring-ai-embeddings call replaced a Document or text body. A float[] body without the text header, a List of float[] with a different number of texts, and a List with an element that is not a Document or a String now fail with an IllegalArgumentException. Before, a vector without its text was stored as the text of float[].toString(), extra vectors or texts were dropped, and other elements were skipped.

SIMILARITY_SEARCH with a float[] body (after spring-ai-embeddings) now searches for the text of the CamelSpringAiEmbeddingInputText header, and fails without it. Before, it searched for the text of float[].toString().

camel-spring-batch - the CamelSpringBatchJobName header needs jobFromHeader=true

The producer now takes the job name from the CamelSpringBatchJobName header only when the endpoint has jobFromHeader=true. Before, the header chose which job was launched also with the default jobFromHeader=false, instead of the job named in the endpoint URI. Set jobFromHeader=true on the endpoint to keep picking the job from the header.

A message header with a null value is no longer turned into a job parameter. Before, such a header failed the job launch, because Spring Batch does not accept a job parameter without a value.

camel-salesforce - a Map or POJO body is sent as JSON, and raw fails on a body it cannot send

The REST operations (such as createSObject, updateSObject and upsertSObject) now marshal a Map or another object that is not an SObject DTO as JSON. Before, they sent its toString(), for example {Name=x}, which is not JSON. Text, bytes, a stream or a file in the body are sent as is, as before.

The raw operation now fails with an InvalidPayloadException when the body cannot be converted to an InputStream, for example a Map. Before, it sent the request with an empty body.

camel-spring-rabbitmq - confirm=auto waits for simple publisher confirms

With the default confirm=auto, an InOnly producer now also waits for the publisher confirm when the connection factory has publisher confirms of type SIMPLE (for example spring.rabbitmq.publisher-confirm-type=simple), as it did for type CORRELATED. Before, it sent such messages without waiting, so a message that RabbitMQ negatively acknowledged, or did not confirm, was lost without an error. Each send now waits up to confirmTimeout (5 seconds by default). It fails with a java.util.concurrent.TimeoutException when RabbitMQ negatively acknowledges the message, and with an org.springframework.amqp.AmqpTimeoutException when RabbitMQ does not confirm it within confirmTimeout. Set confirm=disabled to send without waiting, as before.

camel-cyberark-vault - verifySsl and certificatePath are applied, and getSecret no longer sets the CamelCyberArkVaultSecretValue header

The verifySsl and certificatePath options of the cyberark-vault endpoint, and the camel.vault.cyberark.verifySsl and camel.vault.cyberark.certificatePath properties of the CyberArk Conjur Vault Property Function, now apply to the HTTPS connections to CyberArk Conjur. Before, they were ignored, and the default trust store of the JVM was always used.

  • With certificatePath, the certificates in that file (PEM or DER) are trusted instead of the default trust store of the JVM, and the endpoint or the function fails to start when the file cannot be read. Remove certificatePath to keep the default trust store of the JVM.

  • With verifySsl=false, neither the certificate nor the host name of the server is checked. The option is now marked as an insecure SSL setting, so the security policy reports camel.component.cyberark-vault.verifySsl=false, which fails the startup with camel.main.profile=prod. Set certificatePath to the certificate of the server or of its CA instead.

The getSecret operation now sets the secret only as the message body, and no longer sets the CamelCyberArkVaultSecretValue header. Before, a createSecret later in the same exchange stored the value of that header instead of its own body. A route that reads the secret from the CamelCyberArkVaultSecretValue header after getSecret has to read the body instead, or copy the body into a variable right after getSecret, for example with .setVariable("mySecret", body()), and read it later with ${variable.mySecret}. A variable stays in the exchange, while producers such as HTTP, JMS or Kafka send the headers on to the next system. The createSecret operation still stores the CamelCyberArkVaultSecretValue header when the route sets it.

camel-rocketmq - the headers are named CamelRocketMQ, and waitForSendResult is true by default

The header names of camel-rocketmq were misspelled CamelRockerMQ…​; they are now CamelRocketMQ…​. The Java field names of RocketMQConstants are unchanged; only the header string values have changed.

Previous value New value

CamelRockerMQTopic

CamelRocketMQTopic

CamelRockerMQTag

CamelRocketMQOverrideTopicName

Routes that use the constants (for example setHeader(RocketMQConstants.OVERRIDE_TOPIC_NAME, …​)) work without changes. Routes that use a header by its literal name (for example CamelRockerMQOverrideTopicName in an XML or YAML route, or ${header.CamelRockerMQKey} in Simple) must be updated to the new name; the previous names are no longer read. The generated Endpoint DSL header accessors are renamed accordingly (rockerMQTopic() → rocketMQTopic(), rockerMQOverrideTopicName() → rocketMQOverrideTopicName(), and so on).

The waitForSendResult option is now true by default. Before, it was false, but the producer still waited for the send result and failed the exchange when the send failed: it only reported the exchange as done synchronously while calling the callback later, which breaks the asynchronous routing contract. The default behaviour is therefore unchanged. With waitForSendResult=false an InOnly exchange now continues as soon as the message is handed to the RocketMQ client, and a failed send is only logged (at WARN) and no longer fails the exchange.

camel-azure-cosmosdb - the consumer starts the change feed processor again when its start fails

When the change feed processor of an azure-cosmosdb consumer cannot start (Cosmos DB cannot be reached, the lease container does not exist yet, a role assignment is not applied yet), the consumer now tries again, after 1 second and then with a backoff of up to 30 seconds, until it starts or the consumer stops; before, the route stayed started and never received a change. The first failure of each start still goes to the ExceptionHandler of the consumer (with bridgeErrorHandler=true it is routed to the error handler once), and each further failed attempt is logged as one WARN line.

Its readiness health check (consumer:<routeId>, with the consumer health checks of Camel enabled, the default) is now the initial state (camel.health.initial-state, DOWN by default) while the first attempt to start runs, and DOWN while the change feed processor cannot start, with the failure of the last attempt; before, it was UP. To leave the check out, as before, set camel.health.exclude-pattern=consumer:<routeId>.

camel-vertx-websocket - consumers on the same host and port must use the same SSLContextParameters

All vertx-websocket consumers on the same host and port are served by one HTTP server, which is created from the sslContextParameters of the first consumer to start. A consumer started later with different sslContextParameters (including one with none while the server uses some, or the other way round) used to start and be served with the first consumer’s settings. Such a consumer now fails to start with an IllegalArgumentException naming the host and port. Give every consumer on a host and port the same SSLContextParameters instance (for example the same bean reference, or the global SSL context parameters), or move consumers that need other settings to another port.

camel-as2 - the asynchronous MDN allow-list matches the host and port

The asyncMdnAllowedHosts option now matches the full authority - host and port - of the asynchronous MDN delivery address, rather than the host alone. Each entry is a host with an optional port in host:port form (IPv6 literals use bracket notation, for example [::1] or [::1]:8443). A bare-host entry (for example partner.example) now matches only the default port for the scheme (80 for http, 443 for https); to deliver to a non-default port, list it explicitly (for example partner.example:8443).

A deployment that lists a bare host and receives asynchronous MDNs on a non-default port must add the port to the entry.

camel-asterisk - QUEUE_STATUS and SIP_PEERS return the events of the list

The QUEUE_STATUS and SIP_PEERS actions of the asterisk producer now wait for the whole list and set the org.asteriskjava.manager.ResponseEvents as the body: getResponse() is the response and getEvents() the events, such as QueueParamsEvent, QueueMemberEvent and PeerEntryEvent. Before, the body was only the ManagerResponse saying that the list will follow, and the events were lost. A route that read that response from the body now finds it in ${body.response}. EXTENSION_STATE still sets the ManagerResponse as the body.

camel-jbang - camel validate and camel overview report the same endpoints no route consumes

camel validate source and camel overview now use the same rules for a direct:, seda: or disruptor: endpoint that a route sends to and no route consumes, so they report the same endpoints on the same project:

  • disruptor: endpoints are checked too, and camel overview no longer treats disruptor:q as seda:q (they are different endpoints at runtime); vm: and direct-vm:, which do not exist in Camel 4, are no longer joined with seda: and direct:.

  • camel validate counts a poll or pollEnrich as consuming its endpoint, and a toD without an expression, an enrich with a constant endpoint, a rest verb, and the dead letter channel of an error handler as sending to it. It reads the flows of an Integration resource, and stays quiet for a Pipe.

  • camel overview stays quiet, as camel validate does, when a route consumes an endpoint only known at runtime (from: direct:{{name}}), and no longer reports a to whose uri holds an expression.

So camel validate can report an endpoint it did not report before.

camel-jbang - the AI file tools stay inside the project directory, also through a symbolic link

The file tools of camel mcp, camel ask and the TUI AI agents (camel_validate_source, camel_get_files, camel_edit_file, camel_write_file) now resolve symbolic links before they check that a file is inside the project directory. A file reached through a link in the project that leads out of it, or through a link to nothing, is refused for reading and for writing, where it used to be read or written. Name the files of such a folder through its real directory instead. An absolute file inside the project directory is now accepted, as the same file named relative to it.

camel-ftp, camel-jsch, camel-mina-sftp - the SFTP and SCP clients negotiate the post-quantum key exchange

camel-ftp and camel-mina-sftp now declare org.bouncycastle:bcprov-jdk18on as a compile dependency, and camel-jsch picks it up through camel-ftp.

Both SSH stacks propose a hybrid post-quantum key exchange ahead of the classical groups - mlkem768x25519-sha256 for jsch; sntrup761x25519-sha512, also under its sntrup761x25519-sha512@openssh.com name, then mlkem768x25519-sha256, mlkem1024nistp384-sha384 and mlkem768nistp256-sha256, in that order, for Apache MINA SSHD - but both implement ML-KEM through the Bouncy Castle lightweight API (jsch can also use the JDK from Java 24 on). On the Java 21 baseline neither had it on the runtime classpath, so the hybrid groups never reached the proposal and every SFTP or SCP connection silently fell back to a classical key exchange.

These endpoints therefore now perform a different key exchange than before against a server that offers a hybrid group. Which one depends on the server: OpenSSH before 9.9 offers only sntrup761x25519-sha512@openssh.com, which mina-sftp negotiates and sftp does not propose unless it is added to keyExchangeProtocols; OpenSSH 9.9 and later also offers mlkem768x25519-sha256, which sftp negotiates, while mina-sftp still negotiates sntrup761x25519-sha512 because it proposes that first. A deployment that must keep the previous behaviour can pin the classical groups with keyExchangeProtocols on sftp and mina-sftp, and with kex on ssh. scp (camel-jsch) has no such option: there the proposal is set for the whole JVM with the jsch.kex system property, for example -Djsch.kex=curve25519-sha256,ecdh-sha2-nistp256,diffie-hellman-group16-sha512. Note that these options replace the proposal rather than adding to it, so an endpoint that already pins a list of classical groups is unaffected - and will not negotiate a post-quantum exchange until a hybrid group is added to that list.

With Bouncy Castle on the classpath, Apache MINA SSHD also registers it as the security provider of all its cryptography in camel-mina-sftp, not only of the key exchange: key factories, key pair generation, key agreement, signatures and digests now come from Bouncy Castle rather than from the JDK providers, which matters on a JVM configured for FIPS. The registration is switched off with the -Dorg.apache.sshd.security.provider.BC.enabled=false system property.

camel-ftp, camel-mina-sftp, camel-jsch, camel-smb, camel-azure-files - checksumFileAlgorithm writes a checksum again

Since 4.18 the producers of the remote file components (ftp, ftps, sftp, mina-sftp, scp, smb, azure-files) accepted checksumFileAlgorithm but wrote no checksum file and did not set the CamelFileChecksum header; when an earlier file producer of the route had set that header, they wrote its checksum, the checksum of another payload. They now calculate the checksum of the bytes they upload, write the checksum file (unless checksumWriteFile=false) and set the header, as in 4.14. A checksum header set before the producer is no longer used by any file producer. scp and azure-files upload the bytes of the body without the charset option, while the checksum applies it: do not set charset with checksumFileAlgorithm there. FTP uploads in ASCII mode unless binary=true, and the server may then change the line ends of the stored file, so its checksum can differ from the checksum of the uploaded bytes.

camel-jsch - the scp producer refuses a file name with a line feed or a NUL character

The scp producer now refuses a file name with a line feed or a NUL character, in the file name or in one of its directories, with a GenericFileOperationFailedException. It does so before it starts scp on the remote host, so nothing is written there. Any other character, such as a carriage return or a tab, is sent as before.

scp sends each name on a protocol line that ends with a line feed. Before, a line feed in the file name ended that line early: the remote scp wrote a file named after the part of the name before the line feed, with the rest of the name at the start of its content, and the exchange did not fail. A line feed in a directory failed the exchange, but left a directory named after the part before it. A NUL character failed the exchange on the remote host, or, with an OpenSSH server before 10.6, cut the name short there. Remove or replace these characters in the CamelFileName header or in the fileName option before the message reaches the producer.

camel-file - a done file with a fixed name is kept for the files a poll left

A done file with a fixed name (such as doneFileName=ready) is no longer deleted while a poll left files of its directory for the next poll (beyond maxMessagesPerPoll, or not started); before, they were left until a new done file was written. Meanwhile the polls consume every file of the directory, as they do whenever the done file is there. This also applies to pollEnrich and ConsumerTemplate.receive, which take one file per call: the done file is now kept until the last file of the directory is received, where before the first received file deleted it. This applies to camel-ftp, camel-sftp, camel-smb and camel-azure-files as well.

camel-file - poll and pollEnrich evaluate the fileName on the current message

poll and pollEnrich with a file based component (file, ftp, ftps, sftp, smb and azure-files) evaluate a dynamic fileName that does not use the file functions on the current message, so fileName=${body} and fileName=${body[customer]}.json read its body. Before, the consumer evaluated it for each file found, with that file as the body, so ${body} was the file found. A fileName that uses the file functions (${file:…​}, ${date:file:…​}) is still evaluated for each file found, and headers, variables and exchange properties work as before. A fileName that read the file found through its headers, such as ${header.CamelFileName}, must use the file functions instead (${file:name}).

camel-flink - the datastream endpoint returns a DataStream with the default collect option

A flink:datastream endpoint whose callback returns a DataStream no longer fails with "collect mode not supported for Flink DataStreams." when collect is not set: the DataStream is the message body and the CamelFlinkDataStream header, as with collect=false. An explicit collect=true still fails. The dataStream and dataStreamCallback options of the flink component are now used by endpoints that do not set them, as dataSet and dataSetCallback already were.

camel-micrometer - a counter increment or decrement that does not give a number fails the exchange

A counter with the increment or decrement option (such as increment=${body} or increment=${header.amount}) added 1 to the counter when the expression did not give a number, for example a Map, a POJO or a null body, or a missing header. It now fails the exchange with an IllegalArgumentException, and the counter is not updated. A counter with neither option still adds 1.

camel-mina-sftp - the local work file is deleted when the exchange is done

With localWorkDirectory, camel-mina-sftp now deletes the local work file of a consumed file when the exchange is done, also when it failed, as camel-sftp and camel-ftp do. Before, every consumed file stayed in the local work directory. A file producer in the route now moves the local work file to its target instead of copying it, while the message body still names the local work file. So a later step that reads the body again, such as a second file producer or a convertBodyTo, fails because the file is gone, and another exchange that keeps the java.io.File body (for example wireTap, seda without waiting, or aggregate) finds it deleted. Convert the body before the first file producer or before handing it on, for example with convertBodyTo(byte[].class) (which reads the file into memory). Routes that cleaned the local work directory themselves can stop doing so.

camel-olingo4 - filterAlreadySeen keeps the type of a complex or collection value

With filterAlreadySeen=true, reading a complex property gave a java.util.List of its ClientProperty instead of the ClientComplexValue, and an empty List when it was read again. A collection property gave a java.util.List of the values not read before instead of a ClientCollectionValue. Now a complex value is one result: it is the ClientComplexValue, or null when the same value was read before. A collection value stays a ClientCollectionValue with only the values not read before. The consumer sends no message for a poll where nothing new is left in the collection, as it already did for an entity set. Routes that read the List must read the Olingo value instead.

camel-openstack - potential breaking change

The four remaining Exchange header constants of SwiftConstants have been renamed to follow the Camel naming convention used by the rest of the component, completing the rename done in 4.21. The Java field names are unchanged; only the header string values have changed.

Constant Previous value New value

SwiftConstants.CONTAINER_METADATA_PREFIX

X-Container-Meta-

CamelOpenstackSwiftContainerMetadata

SwiftConstants.VERSIONS_LOCATION

X-Versions-Location

CamelOpenstackSwiftVersionsLocation

SwiftConstants.CONTAINER_READ

X-Container-Read

CamelOpenstackSwiftContainerRead

SwiftConstants.CONTAINER_WRITE

X-Container-Write

CamelOpenstackSwiftContainerWrite

The previous values were kept in 4.21 on the understanding that they were part of the Swift HTTP contract. They are not: the container producer passes the values to the openstack4j CreateUpdateContainerOptions, which builds the X-Container-Meta-, X-Versions-Location, X-Container-Read and X-Container-Write names of the Swift request itself. The request Camel sends to Swift is therefore unchanged by this rename.

Routes that reference the constants symbolically (for example setHeader(SwiftConstants.CONTAINER_READ, …​)) continue to work without changes. Routes that set one of these headers by its literal string value (for example setHeader("X-Container-Read", …​) in an XML or YAML route) must be updated to the new value (setHeader("CamelOpenstackSwiftContainerRead", …​)); the previous names are no longer read.

The generated Endpoint DSL header accessors on the Swift HeaderNameBuilder are renamed accordingly (xContainerMeta() → openstackSwiftContainerMetadata(), xVersionsLocation() → openstackSwiftVersionsLocation(), xContainerRead() → openstackSwiftContainerRead(), xContainerWrite() → openstackSwiftContainerWrite()).

camel-core - Split and Multicast child producers start with their route

Split and Multicast EIPs now start their child output producers during the route’s own start, instead of during CamelContext initialization. The visible changes are:

  • The producers now start when the route starts, not during CamelContext initialization. A CamelContext that is only built or initialized (without starting routes) no longer starts them.

  • Each route’s producers now start in startupOrder with their route, instead of ahead of all routes.

  • The producers of an autoStartup=false route are no longer started when the context starts; they start when the route itself is started (regardless of which route controller is used).

camel-core - Forced route-stop cut-off marks exchange as interrupted in any nested EIP

An exchange that a forced route stop cuts off inside a nested EIP (split, multicast, filter, choice, …​) of the stopped route is now marked as interrupted (exchange.getExchangeExtension().setInterrupted(true)) after the cut-off WARN is logged. This prevents nested stopped error handlers from each emitting their own WARN for the same exchange. The interrupted flag also causes the error handler to skip redelivery, onException routes, and the dead letter channel — the same behavior that DirectProducer applies (CAMEL-25502) when a route is stopped while an exchange is waiting for a consumer. A calling route that is still running (for example through direct) then also no longer runs its onException, redelivery or dead letter channel for that exchange. If your application relied on onException or the dead letter channel handling a RouteStoppingException from a nested EIP of the stopped route, it will no longer trigger on a forced route stop.

camel-opentelemetry-metrics - a counter increment or decrement that does not give a number fails the exchange

A counter with the increment or decrement option (such as increment=${body} or increment=${header.amount}) added 1 to the counter when the expression did not give a number, for example a Map, a POJO or a null body, or a missing header. It now fails the exchange with an IllegalArgumentException, and the counter is not updated. A counter with neither option still adds 1. The CamelMetricsCounterIncrement and CamelMetricsCounterDecrement headers are documented as long, which is how they are read.

camel-optaplanner - one SolverManager per endpoint, and consumers get the events of their own CamelContext

With configFile and no SolverManager (neither the solverManager option nor the CamelOptaPlannerSolverManager header), the producer created a new SolverManager for every exchange and never closed it, so its threads were never released. The endpoint now creates one SolverManager on first use and closes it when the endpoint stops. As the exchanges of the endpoint share it, solving a problem while another one with the same problemId is still being solved now fails with The problemId (1) is already solving, as it already did with a SolverManager you configure. Use a different problemId (an endpoint per problem) to solve several problems at the same time.

A consumer now gets the best solutions of the producers of its own CamelContext only. Before, it also got the ones of producers in any other CamelContext of the JVM with the same problemId. A consumer now also gets the best solutions of a producer that solves synchronously (async=false), not only of the ones that solve asynchronously.

camel-file - the checksum of checksumFileAlgorithm is the checksum of the written file

With checksumFileAlgorithm, the file producer calculated the checksum from the body before it was written. With charset set, it also decoded that body with the default charset of the JVM instead of the charset of the exchange. So with charset, the checksum (the CamelFileChecksum header and the checksum file) did not match the written file as soon as the encoding changed a byte, a body read with another charset (such as the bytes of a file consumed with charset=ISO-8859-1) was written with its non-ASCII characters replaced, and a String body could fail with an UnmappableCharacterException; with fileExist=Append and appendChars, the checksum left out the appended characters. The checksum is now calculated from the bytes written to the file, and the body is decoded as it is without checksumFileAlgorithm. Checksums of such files differ from the ones written before.

camel-file - path separator normalization for getFile/directory consistency

On Linux and macOS, a backslash in the file: URI path (e.g. file:target\subdir) is now normalized to a forward slash, so that getFile().getPath() and getConfiguration().getDirectory() return the same value. Previously, getFile() preserved the backslash while getDirectory() replaced it with /, which could cause the producer to create a spurious directory with a backslash in its name and the consumer to miss files in a backslash-named directory. On Windows, where java.io.File already normalizes both separators to \, this change has no effect.

camel-flatpack - with splitRows each message has its own row, and a failing row fails the producer

With splitRows=true (the default), every row message shared the one parsed DataSet, so the next row moved the row of a message that was not yet converted. The body of each row message is now its own read-only DataSet with only that row (org.apache.camel.component.flatpack.DataSetRow): it converts to a Map as before, converting it to a List gives that one row instead of all the rows, and the methods that change the DataSet (such as setValue or remove) throw an UnsupportedOperationException.

An exception in the route that consumes the rows was lost, and the producer carried on with the next rows. The exception now fails the producer, and the rows after the failing row are not sent.

Converting a DataSet that is not on a row (the whole DataSet with splitRows=false) to a Map now fails with an IllegalStateException instead of a NullPointerException; convert it to a List.

camel-ftp, camel-mina-sftp - the SFTP consumers resolve listed symbolic links for jailStartingDirectory

With jailStartingDirectory enabled (the default), the sftp and mina-sftp consumers now resolve a listed file that the server reports as a symbolic link, and skip it when its target is outside the starting directory or cannot be resolved (such as a dangling link), as the file consumer does. A link whose target contains a backslash (\) is skipped too. Before, such a link was consumed like any other file, wherever it pointed. A skipped file is logged at WARN the first time and at DEBUG on later polls. Links resolving inside the starting directory work as before. Set jailStartingDirectory=false to keep following links to other directories.

camel-pg-replication-slot - the LSN of a message is in the CamelPgReplicationSlotLsn header

The consumer no longer sets the exchange id to the log sequence number (LSN) of the message. Several messages can have the same LSN (for example the BEGIN and the first change of a transaction), so exchange ids repeated. The exchange has a generated exchange id now, as with any other consumer, and the LSN is in the new CamelPgReplicationSlotLsn header (a String such as 0/16B3748). Use ${header.CamelPgReplicationSlotLsn} instead of ${exchangeId} to read the LSN.

camel-pqc - symmetricKeyLength defaults to 256

The symmetricKeyLength option of the pqc component and of the pqc data format now defaults to 256 instead of 128. The default KEM is ML-KEM-768, a NIST category 3 algorithm producing a 256-bit shared secret, and deriving a 128-bit key from it threw that margin away.

A route that leaves symmetricKeyLength unset therefore now produces a 256-bit key. Two consequences:

  • A key encapsulated by 4.24 cannot be used by a peer still running the old default, and material encrypted with the pqc data format by one version cannot be read by the other. Set symmetricKeyLength=128 explicitly on both ends while they are mixed.

  • The algorithms that cannot take a 256-bit key now fail unless symmetricKeyLength is set explicitly. Those are RC2 and CAST5 (40, 64, 128), DESEDE (128, 192), and GRAIN128, HC128, SEED and SM4 (128 only). Conversely CHACHA7539, GOST28147, GOST3412-2015 and HC256 accept 256 only, and no longer need an explicit value.

    The pqc component validates the length when the route starts and fails there with Invalid symmetric key length 256 for algorithm <name>. Valid key lengths: […​].

    The pqc data format does not check the length when it starts, so it fails on the first message instead, when the cipher rejects the key. Of the algorithms the data format accepts, only SEED and SM4 are affected. Note also that the algorithm itself can come from the CamelPQCSymmetricAlgorithm header, so a route whose configured algorithm is fine can still fail per message on an override.

camel-pqc - an unknown hybridKdfAlgorithm is rejected

The hybrid KEM operations now fail with an IllegalArgumentException when hybridKdfAlgorithm is not one of HKDF-SHA256, HKDF-SHA384 or HKDF-SHA512. Before, any other value silently fell back to HKDF-SHA256, so a typo such as HKDF-SHA-512 went unnoticed - and if only one end of an exchange carried the typo, both ends still reported success while deriving the key with different digests.

The HKDF info bytes of the hybrid KEM are also now encoded explicitly as UTF-8, where they previously used the platform default charset. The info string is the symmetric algorithm name, so this has no effect for the usual values such as AES, which are pure ASCII. A deployment running on a JVM whose default charset cannot encode that name identically to UTF-8 would, however, derive a different key than the previous version, and could not decrypt material produced by it.

camel-pqc - a stateful signature key is claimed for one process

XMSS, XMSSMT, LMS and HSS are one-time-signature schemes: signing twice from the same index breaks the key. The index lives inside the private key, so two processes that load the same key from a shared store - two pods on one volume, two replicas reading one vault secret - each advance their own copy and hand out the same index.

Before it first signs with a stateful key, the pqc producer now claims it from the configured KeyLifecycleManager, through the new KeyLifecycleManager.claimStatefulKey(String) and releaseStatefulKey(String) methods. Both have a default implementation, so a custom manager still compiles; the default grants no claim.

  • FileBasedKeyLifecycleManager takes an exclusive FileLock on a <keyId>.lock file in the key directory, so a second process reading that directory is refused.

  • InMemoryKeyLifecycleManager grants the claim - its keys never leave the process.

  • The vault-backed managers (AWS Secrets Manager, Azure Key Vault, Hashicorp Vault) cannot offer a lease Camel can take, so they do not grant it. An endpoint signing with a stateful key through one of them now fails, with a message naming the key.

Set the new allowSharedStatefulKey=true option on the endpoint to sign anyway, when a single signer is guaranteed outside Camel (one replica, an external lock, a leader election). A warning is logged once per key.

Nothing changes for the stateless signature algorithms (ML-DSA, SLH-DSA, Falcon and the rest), for the KEM operations, or for an endpoint that signs with a stateful key and no KeyLifecycleManager - that case persists no index at all and keeps the warning it already had.

camel-pqc - the BouncyCastle providers stay registered when an endpoint stops

Stopping a pqc endpoint no longer removes the BC and BCPQC providers from java.security.Security.

java.security.Security is JVM-global, and the endpoint only added a provider when it was absent, so stopping one endpoint could remove a provider it had not installed - breaking the other pqc endpoint of the same route, other CamelContext instances, components such as camel-crypto, camel-ssh and camel-xmlsecurity, and any non-Camel code in the same JVM.

An application that relied on the providers being de-registered on shutdown must now remove them itself with Security.removeProvider("BC") and Security.removeProvider("BCPQC").

camel-printer - a body that does not convert to a stream fails the exchange

The lpr producer submitted a print job with no data, without an error, for a message body that has no conversion to InputStream, such as a Map, a POJO or a number, and for a message without a body. It now fails the exchange with an InvalidPayloadException. Send the document as text, bytes, a stream or a file.

camel-printer - a doc flavor the print service does not support fails the exchange

The lpr producer skipped the print job, without an error, when the print service did not support the doc flavor of the flavor and mimeType options, and the exchange completed as if the document was printed. It now fails the exchange with a javax.print.PrintException that names the doc flavor and the print service, also with sendToPrinter=false. Set the flavor and mimeType options to a doc flavor that the printer supports.

camel-debezium - the row flattened by ExtractNewRecordState is the body

With the Debezium ExtractNewRecordState single message transformation (ExtractNewDocumentState for MongoDB), the body is now the flattened row (or document) as a Struct. Before, the body was null, as the component only read the after field of the change event, which the transformation removes. Any record whose value is a Struct without an after field is now the body, except the events of Debezium itself (schema change, heartbeat and transaction metadata events), which keep a null body. The CamelDebeziumOperation header is read from the __op field or header that the transformation adds with add.fields=op or add.headers=op.

The debezium-mongodb consumer no longer reads the patch field for an update, which Debezium removed in 2.0. The changed fields of an update are now in the new CamelDebeziumUpdateDescription header.

The Struct to Map type converter now also converts a Struct inside a list or a map.

camel-debezium-mysql - a signed TINYINT column is a Byte

Debezium has been upgraded to 3.7.0.Final, which maps a signed MySQL TINYINT column to the Kafka Connect INT8 type instead of INT16 (debezium/dbz#2235). In the Struct of the message body, and in the Map it converts to, the value of such a column is now a Byte instead of a Short. Code that casts the value to Short must read it as a Number (or a Byte) instead. A TINYINT UNSIGNED column is still a Short.

The new Debezium options of 3.7 are available on all debezium-* components. The ddlParserType option of debezium-mysql is kept, although Debezium 3.7 no longer lists it in the configuration of its MySQL connector.

camel-docker - the streaming operations give their result, and their failures fail the exchange

The operations imagebuild, imagepull, imagepush, containerattach, containerlog, containerwait and execstart now fail the exchange when they fail. Before, an error such as a missing build context was only logged and the exchange completed with the body unchanged. A failed build or push, reported by Docker in the output of the operation, now also fails the exchange.

The body after these operations changed:

  • imagebuild: the id of the built image, also for a build with a tag. Before, it was null for a tagged build. The body can now also be a byte[] or another body that converts to an InputStream (the tar archive of the build context), besides an InputStream or a java.io.File.

  • containerattach, containerlog and execstart: the output of stdout and stderr as a String. Before, the body was the docker-java callback, and the output was lost.

  • containerwait: the exit code of the container as an Integer. Before, it was the docker-java callback.

  • imagepull and imagepush: the last PullResponseItem or PushResponseItem of the progress. Before, it was the docker-java callback.

The events consumer now gives the start of the event stream to Docker in seconds, as the Docker API expects, so the past events of CamelDockerInitialRange (in milliseconds) are received. Before, the time was in milliseconds.

DockerConstants.DEFAULT_CMD_EXEC_FACTORY now names the Netty factory, which is the default of the cmdExecFactory option. Before, it named the Jersey factory. The default itself did not change; camel-docker now adds the Netty native transports for arm64, so the default works on Apple silicon and Linux on arm64.

camel-pubnub - FIRE and SETSTATE with an empty body fail without calling PubNub

The FIRE and SETSTATE operations with an empty body now fail with a CamelException (Can not fire empty message or Can not set state with an empty body) and do not call PubNub, as PUBLISH does. Before, the call to PubNub still ran, so FIRE with a null body could end with the PubNubException Message Missing instead. The message of the SETSTATE failure was Can not publish empty message.

The PNMessageResult body of the consumer now converts to a String: a string message as its text, any other message as JSON. Before, the conversion gave the toString() of the PNMessageResult object.

camel-jbang - camel validate normalize writes one file per input file to an output directory

camel validate normalize <files> --output=<dir> now writes one normalized file per input file into the directory, with the name of the input file, instead of merging all the routes into normalized.yaml. A top-level onException of a file then keeps applying to the routes of that file only, as it does in the source. This applies to a single input file too: camel validate normalize orders.yaml --output=out/ now writes out/orders.yaml instead of out/normalized.yaml. normalized.yaml is only written for what was not loaded from one of the input files. Input files with the same name in different directories are written as <parent directory>-<name>. Scripts that read <dir>/normalized.yaml must read the per-file outputs instead.

The command now refuses to write over one of its input files (for example --output=. in the directory of the routes) and exits with an error.

With --output=<file> or without --output, the routes are still merged, and the command now warns, on the standard error, when the top-level onException of one file applies to the routes of the other files in the merged output.

camel-pqc - generateSecretKeyEncapsulation stamps the JCE algorithm name on the secret key

The SecretKeyWithEncapsulation produced by generateSecretKeyEncapsulation now carries the mapped JCE algorithm name, as the extractSecretKeyEncapsulation and extractSecretKeyFromEncapsulation operations already did. Before, it carried the raw enum name.

This is only visible for the two algorithms whose enum name differs from the JCE name: DESEDE now yields DESede (was DESEDE), and GOST3412_2015 now yields GOST3412-2015 (was GOST3412_2015). The raw names are not resolvable cipher algorithms, so a key labelled with one could not be passed to Cipher.getInstance(key.getAlgorithm()), and the generate and extract ends of the same exchange disagreed on the key’s identity. Code asserting on the old value must be updated.

generateSecretKeyEncapsulation now also rejects a missing symmetricKeyAlgorithm with Symmetric Algorithm needs to be specified, as the extract operations already did, instead of failing later inside BouncyCastle with Missing argument.

camel-pqc - the hybrid KEM operations stamp the JCE algorithm name too

hybridGenerateSecretKeyEncapsulation and hybridExtractSecretKeyEncapsulation label the SecretKey they produce with the mapped JCE algorithm name, as the non-hybrid operations do. DESEDE now yields DESede and GOST3412_2015 now yields GOST3412-2015; every other algorithm is unaffected, since its configured name and its JCE name are the same.

Only the label changes. The derived key itself is unchanged: the configured name is also the HKDF info the two shared secrets are combined with, and that keeps its original value, so an encapsulation produced by an earlier version still decrypts.

camel-jbang - camel ask asks before a call that changes something, reaches out or runs code

camel ask now asks on the terminal before a file write (showing the diff), a message to an endpoint the integration does not have, SQL that changes data, an expression in a language that runs code, stopping or restarting the integration, and a camel command that changes something. Without a terminal to ask on, as in a script, such a call does not run: pass --yes to let it run without asking.

The model gets the shared file tools (camel_get_files, camel_edit_file, camel_write_file), which validate what they write, instead of the read_file, list_files and write_file tools of camel ask. A local model gets a core set of tools (--tools=auto, the default), a hosted model the full set. The default of --max-iterations is now 25 instead of 10, and --model llama3.2 now selects that model also when a model better at tool calling, such as qwen3.6, is installed.

camel-pqc - the usage-count and PENDING_ROTATION key rotation signals now fire

Two of the three documented rotation signals of KeyRotationScheduler previously never triggered. Both now work, so a deployment that already configured them will start rotating keys where before nothing happened.

  • maxKeyUsage (and camel.component.pqc.key-rotation-max-usage) read usageCount, which was only advanced for the stateful signature algorithms (XMSS, XMSSMT, LMS, HSS). For ML-DSA, SLH-DSA, Falcon and the KEM keys the counter stayed at zero forever. A signing operation now advances it for every key, provided a keyLifecycleManager is configured and the exchange carries a CamelPQCKeyId header.

  • The scheduler’s default key filter accepted only ACTIVE keys, so a key an operator had marked PENDING_ROTATION was skipped before needsRotation() was consulted. The default filter now accepts ACTIVE and PENDING_ROTATION. A custom filter set with setKeyFilter is unaffected.

Review any configured maxKeyUsage threshold before upgrading: a long-running deployment may rotate on the first check once the counter starts advancing.

Recording the usage also costs a write. A signing endpoint with a keyLifecycleManager and a CamelPQCKeyId header now updates the key metadata in the backing store on every signature, where a non-stateful key previously wrote nothing. Against AWS Secrets Manager, Azure Key Vault or HashiCorp Vault that is a remote call per message. A failure to read or write that metadata does not fail the exchange for a non-stateful key - it is logged once per producer and the signature is returned - because losing a usage count only delays a rotation. Leave the CamelPQCKeyId header off the signing exchanges, or use maxKeyAge or nextRotationAt, if the write is not wanted.

The key encapsulation operations do not record usage, so maxKeyUsage still never triggers for a KEM key.

camel-pqc - a failed stateful signature no longer carries the signature headers

Signing with one of the stateful hash-based algorithms (LMS, HSS, XMSS, XMSSMT) consumes a one-time index inside the private key. That advanced key is now persisted through the keyLifecycleManager before the signature is placed on the message, instead of after.

Such an exchange already failed when the key state could not be stored, so what changes is the content of the failed message: it no longer carries CamelPQCSignature, nor the hybrid CamelPQCHybridSignature, CamelPQCClassicalSignature and CamelPQCPqcSignature headers.
