A route that caught the failure with onException(…​).handled(true), or routed it to a dead letter channel, could previously read the signature off the failed message and pass it on; it can no longer do so. That is the point of the change - a signature whose index advance was never recorded must not escape, because after a restart the key resumes at the previous index and can sign a second time with an index already used.

Signing a stateful key without a keyLifecycleManager, and signing any key without a CamelPQCKeyId header or with no stored metadata for the key, now logs a warning once per producer rather than being silently ignored. A non-stateful key without a keyLifecycleManager stays silent, as there is no maxKeyUsage threshold to miss. For a stateful key the warning names the index-reuse risk; for any other key it names the usageCount that will not advance.

camel-jooq - a message with no body fails

The jooq producer now fails with an InvalidPayloadException when the message body is null and the query option is not set. Before, operation NONE (the default) created a new, empty record of the entityType and stored it, so a message with no body inserted an empty row (or failed with a database constraint error). Operations EXECUTE and FETCH failed with a NoSuchMethodException for org.jooq.Query.<init>(). Send the record, or the org.jooq.Query, in the message body. The operation option also no longer lists CONSUME, which was never a valid value.

camel-jsonpath - easy predicates with <= and >= match the boundary value

An easy predicate (such as store.book.price <= 10 or $.store.book.price >= 10) now matches the boundary value. Before, price <= 10 ran as price < 10 and price >= 10 as price > 10, so a message with a price of exactly 10 was not matched, and a content-based router sent it to another branch. A route that worked around this (for example with price <= 10.01) can go back to the plain boundary. Also, an operator word inside a quoted value (title == 'Java in Action') is now part of the value, and the type-safe operators === and !== are passed to JsonPath as written (before, they ran as == and !=).

camel-jbang - camel get endpoint shows the scope of an endpoint

camel get endpoint replaces the REMOTE column with SCOPE, which tells three kinds of endpoint apart: remote (an external system, as before), internal (Camel plumbing that stays inside the integration, such as direct, seda or log, as the catalog marks its component internal) and local (neither, such as exec or bean). Scripts that read the REMOTE column of the table must read SCOPE instead. The --json output keeps remote and adds internal.

camel-pqc - the CamelPQCOperation header is validated, and two header types are corrected

A CamelPQCOperation header selecting an operation the endpoint holds no key material for is now rejected with an IllegalArgumentException naming the requested operation, the configured one, and what is missing. Before, an override crossing between the signature and the key encapsulation operations dereferenced a null and surfaced as a bare NullPointerException. Routes relying on such an override have to configure a separate endpoint for the other operation; the override itself keeps working within a family and for the operations that need no key material.

Two header declarations also carried the wrong javaType in the component metadata, which is visible to tooling and in the generated header table. CamelPQCOperation is org.apache.camel.component.pqc.PQCOperations (was String) and CamelPQCSecretKey is javax.crypto.SecretKey (was Boolean). The runtime values are unchanged - only the declared types were wrong.

camel-jbang - finding integrations by name or PID

All commands find running integrations the same way:

  • A PID given to a command must be a running Camel integration (a live process with a status file in ~/.camel). Before, any PID was used as is, so camel stop 1234 --kill killed whatever process had that PID.

  • The commands no longer find an integration whose Camel has stopped (phase 9 or later), such as the launcher process of an integration that runs in a child process. camel ps does not list it anymore.

  • The --timeout of camel cmd send, camel cmd receive, camel eval expression, camel transform message and the camel semantic commands also takes a duration such as 30s (a number is still millis).

camel-pqc - KeyLifecycleManager behaviour aligned across the implementations

Four differences between the KeyLifecycleManager implementations are resolved. All four are corrections, but each is observable.

  • updateKeyMetadata on the AWS Secrets Manager, Azure Key Vault and HashiCorp Vault managers no longer re-stores the key pair. It previously read the key back and wrote it again, creating a new version of the private key secret on every status change - with the default max_versions = 10 of a Vault KV v2 mount, ten metadata edits purged every historical version of the private key. It also meant revokeKey threw for a key whose private secret was already gone. The file-based manager already wrote metadata only.

  • FileBasedKeyLifecycleManager.listKeys() now scans the key directory instead of returning an in-process cache filled once at construction. A key file written after start-up - by another process, a restored backup or a sidecar - was previously invisible, which silently stopped KeyRotationScheduler from rotating it.

  • The deleteKeyState operation now fails with IllegalStateException when no keyLifecycleManager is configured, as every other key lifecycle operation already did. It previously reported success having deleted nothing.

  • exportKey with PEM and includePrivate = true produces a document that importKey can read back. The combined export carries both a PUBLIC KEY and a PRIVATE KEY block, and the parser stripped only one pair of armour lines, leaving the other in the Base64 input and failing with Illegal base64 character 2d. The export format itself is unchanged, so documents written by earlier versions are unaffected.

  • importKey resolves the KeyFactory with the BouncyCastle provider that serves the algorithm, as the file-based manager’s key loading already did. On JDK 24 and later the JDK itself implements ML-DSA and ML-KEM and is listed before BouncyCastle, so the provider-less lookup used until now returned a JDK key, and signing with it through the component’s BouncyCastle Signature failed with unknown private key passed to ML-DSA. A key imported through the AWS Secrets Manager, Azure Key Vault, HashiCorp Vault or in-memory manager is now a BouncyCastle key on every JDK.

PEM handling is also now explicitly UTF-8 rather than the platform default charset.

camel-jbang - exit codes, and errors on stderr

The Camel CLI has a documented set of exit codes (see Exit Codes and Output): 0 success, 1 failed, 2 usage error, 3 not found, 66 file not found, 70 internal error and 124 timed out. A script that checks the exit code of a command must take these changes into account:

  • A command that ends with an unexpected exception exits with 70 instead of 1, and prints ERROR:, the message and the stack trace on stderr. Add --verbose or set CAMEL_CLI_DEBUG=true to print the stack trace of other errors too.

  • Commands that exited with 255 now exit with 1, 2, 3, 66 or 124: camel bind, camel debug, camel dependency update, camel eval expression, camel infra log, camel transform message and camel update run. camel debug exits with the exit code of the debugged integration when it ends.

  • camel semantic exits with 124 instead of 4 when the integration does not reply in time.

  • camel generate schema exits with 2 for an empty argument, 3 when the class is not found, 1 when the schema cannot be generated and 70 for an unexpected error, instead of 4, 2, 3 and 4.

  • camel transform dataweave exits with 2 without --input or --expression, and with 66 when the input path does not exist or holds no .dwl files, instead of 1.

  • camel cmd sql exits with 124 instead of 1 when the query does not answer in time.

  • A source file of camel cmd route-diagram, camel cmd route-structure and camel cmd route-topology that does not exist exits with 66 instead of 1. The parts of a Java route the parser could not read are printed as a warning on stderr (also with --json, which lists them in unread as before).

  • A file or directory that does not exist exits with 66 instead of 1: a source file or --source-dir of camel run, the route file of camel explain and camel harden (checked before the LLM is contacted), and the pom.xml of camel dependency runtime.

  • A name that does not exist exits with 3 instead of 1: the Kamelet of camel init --from-kamelet, the service of camel infra run, the key of camel config get, the name given to camel doc, and the plugin of camel plugin delete.

  • camel cmd memory-leak and camel restart exit with 3 when no running integration, or more than one, matches the name. camel cmd memory-leak exits with 124 when the integration does not reply in time.

  • camel init without a file, or with --from-kamelet and a file that is not a .kamelet.yaml file, camel infra run with a malformed property, and camel kubernetes delete without --name exit with 2 instead of 1.

  • A command that runs its default sub-command (camel config, camel dependency, camel get, camel infra, camel plugin, camel top, camel update and camel version) exits with the exit code of that sub-command, instead of always 0.

  • The first use of the command of a Camel plugin (such as camel kubernetes) installs the plugin and runs the command, instead of asking to run the command again and exiting with 0.

Errors, warnings and tips are printed on stderr instead of stdout, so stdout only has the output of the command, such as JSON. If a script reads the error text of a command from stdout, read stderr instead. The list of plugins that are not installed, after camel and camel --help (no longer after the help of a command, such as camel run --help), is printed on stderr, and only in an interactive terminal.

camel-pqc - parameterSpec accepts MAYO and SNOVA, and the FIPS advisory no longer flags two aliases

parameterSpec can now select a named parameter set for MAYO and SNOVA. Both were rejected on the grounds that they have no name-addressable BouncyCastle parameter spec, which is not the case: MayoParameterSpec.fromName and SnovaParameterSpec.fromName both exist, and neither algorithm is stateful. The stateful hash-based signatures (XMSS, XMSSMT, LMS, HSS) are still rejected, as they genuinely have no such spec.

DILITHIUM and SPHINCSPLUS no longer produce the "not part of NIST FIPS 204/205" warning on hybrid endpoints. Both are aliases that resolve to ML-DSA and SLH-DSA, so the warning was recommending the algorithm already in use. FALCON, MAYO, SNOVA, XMSS and XMSSMT still warn.

The five default key material classes that generated their key pair with the provider’s default RNG (Falcon, MAYO, SNOVA, LMS, HSS) now use the shared SecureRandomHelper like the other sixteen. No key format or parameter set changes; only the randomness source is now uniform.

camel-docker - the connection and credential headers need the allowConnectionAndCredentialsFromHeaders option

The producer of the docker component took the Docker daemon to connect to, how to connect to it, and the registry credentials from the CamelDockerHost, CamelDockerPort, CamelDockerSocketEnabled, CamelDockerSecure, CamelDockerTlsVerify, CamelDockerCertPath, CamelDockerCmdExecFactory, CamelDockerUsername, CamelDockerPassword and CamelDockerServerAddress headers when they were set, over the options of the endpoint. These headers are now ignored unless the endpoint sets the new allowConnectionAndCredentialsFromHeaders=true option (disabled by default), and the producer uses the host, port, socket, secure, tlsVerify, certPath, cmdExecFactory, username, password and serverAddress options of the endpoint.

A route that relies on these headers, for example one that sends the registry username and password as headers to docker:auth or docker:imagepush, must set allowConnectionAndCredentialsFromHeaders=true on the endpoint, or on the component (camel.component.docker.allowConnectionAndCredentialsFromHeaders=true) to apply it to all its endpoints.

The other headers, such as CamelDockerContainerId, CamelDockerEmail and CamelDockerRequestTimeout, still take precedence over the options of the endpoint, as before. The events and stats consumers do not change: they always used the options of the endpoint.

camel-docker - imagepull and imagepush authenticate only to the registry of serverAddress

The imagepull and imagepush operations of the docker component added the registry authentication of the endpoint (username, password, email and serverAddress) to every pull and push, whatever registry the image reference names. They now add it only when the registry host and port of the image (Docker Hub for an image without a registry host, such as ubuntu or team/app) match those of serverAddress. An image in another registry is pulled or pushed without authentication. Set serverAddress to the registry of the images, or use one endpoint per registry.

Without a username on the endpoint, imagepull and imagepush failed with a NullPointerException ("Configured username is null."). They now pull or push without authentication.

camel-cli-connector - every action with a request id gets an answer

An action written to ~/.camel/<pid>-action-<requestId>.json now always gets an answer in ~/.camel/<pid>-output-<requestId>.json, so a tool no longer waits for its timeout:

  • an action without a result (such as a route stop) answers {"status":"success"},

  • an action that is unknown, throws an exception or reports an error answers {"status":"failed","error":"…​"}, with "reason":"not-found" when what it was asked to work on does not exist (such as No route matching: route9, and the new No processor matching: …​).

The WebSocket transport adds the same reason to its result frame. The shared ~/.camel/<pid>-action.json used by older Camel CLI versions is unchanged. A tool built on the file transport that relied on actions without a result writing nothing, or that waited for a timeout, sees these answers now. The Camel MCP route tools, for example, now report a route stop as done instead of a timeout.

camel-jbang - JSON output

With --json, stdout is exactly one JSON document, also when the command fails, so a script or an AI agent can parse it (see JSON output):

  • An error prints {"status":"error","code":3,"message":"…​"} on stdout, with the exit code of the command in code, and the ERROR: line on stderr as before. Before, stdout was empty or had text.

  • A list with nothing in it prints [] instead of nothing, such as camel ps, camel get …​ and camel top …​ when no integration is running, and camel catalog …​ when the filter matches nothing. The message about a filter that matches nothing is printed on stderr.

  • camel top route --json and camel top group --json print JSON instead of a table, with the load averages in load01, load05 and load15.

  • camel get route --error --json prints the routes with an error, and their lastError, as JSON instead of tables. camel get health --trace --json puts the stackTrace and metadata of a check in the JSON, instead of printing them as text after it.

  • camel get count --total --json and camel get count --fail --json print JSON instead of the comma separated counts.

  • camel infra get --json and camel infra stop --json print JSON instead of text, camel infra run --background --json prints the pid as JSON, and camel infra run --json only prints the properties of the service. camel infra get and camel infra stop only see services that are running, and exit with 3 when a name matches none.

  • camel overview --format=json only prints the JSON on stdout: the Saved message of --save and --ai, and the progress and the prompts of --ai, go to stderr, and --ai no longer prints the summary before the JSON. camel overview --apply-descriptions --format=json prints the changed files as JSON.

  • camel doctor exits with 1 when a check fails (Maven cannot resolve artifacts, or more than one installation of the Camel CLI is found). A missing container runtime or Ollama, and ports in use, are warnings, which do not fail it.

New --json options: camel doctor (the status OK, WARN or FAIL of each check), camel validate yaml, camel validate properties and camel validate source (valid, file and errors of each file, as the MCP tool camel_validate_source answers), camel doc (the documentation the MCP tool camel_catalog_doc gives), camel config get, camel config list, camel plugin get and camel plugin list.

camel-jbang - camel cmd actions report failures and timeouts

The commands that act on a running integration now report how the action went in their exit code (see Exit Codes and Output):

  • An action without a result waits until the integration confirms it, and exits with 1 when it fails, 3 when the route or processor does not exist, and 124 when the integration does not answer within --timeout (10 seconds by default; a number is millis, or use a duration such as 30s): camel cmd start-route, stop-route, suspend-route, resume-route, start-group, stop-group, enable-processor, disable-processor, gc, reload, reset-stats, logger --logging-level and camel trace --action=start/stop. Before, they wrote the action and exited with 0. An integration on an older Camel version takes the action without confirming it.

  • A command that waits for a result exits with 124 when the integration does not answer in time, and with 1 when it answers with an error, instead of a message and 1, or nothing and 0: such as camel cmd thread-dump, heap-histogram, heap-dump, browse, span, stub, sql, load, memory-leak, route-dump, route-structure, route-diagram, route-topology, camel get bean, source, route-controller, startup-recorder, camel top source, camel get error --diagram and camel get history --diagram. When the integration has no data to show (a thread dump, the route controller, top source), or OpenTelemetry is not enabled (camel cmd span), the command exits with 1 instead of 0.

  • A name or pid that matches no running integration exits with 3, instead of doing nothing and exiting with 0. Without a name (all integrations) and nothing running, the command exits with 0 and prints No running Camel integrations on stderr.

  • camel eval expression with a name or pid that matches no running integration exits with 3, instead of starting a new Camel to evaluate in: only without a name, or with --isolated, a new Camel evaluates the expression. An evaluation that fails exits with 1, and the Using existing running Camel integration line is printed on stderr.

  • camel cmd send exits with 1 when the exchange failed and 124 when the endpoint did not answer in time, also without a name (sending from a local Camel), which exited with 0 before. camel transform message exits with 1 when the transformation fails and 124 when it does not answer in time.

  • camel cmd load without --source, a header that is not in the key=value form (camel cmd send, camel eval expression, camel transform message), and an unknown --action (camel cmd receive, camel trace) exit with 2.

  • The --filter of camel cmd route-dump, camel cmd route-structure and camel get source takes several names separated by comma, as its description says, and a filter that matches no route exits with 3.

The commands that wait for the integration have a --timeout option, and the ones that return data have a --json option: camel cmd thread-dump, heap-histogram, heap-dump, browse, span, stub, sql, logger, route-dump, camel get bean, source, route-controller, startup-recorder, camel top source and camel trace (the status of tracing). camel get history --json prints one JSON array instead of one document per line.

camel-jbang - camel get, top and ps: not found, unreadable status, and the JSON of the listings

  • camel get …​, camel top …​ and camel ps with a name or pid that matches no running integration exit with 3, instead of printing nothing and exiting with 0. Without a name and with nothing running, they exit with 0 and print No running Camel integrations on stderr (and [] with --json).

  • An integration whose status file a listing cannot read, such as one of an older Camel version without a value the listing needs, is skipped with a WARN: on stderr, instead of failing the listing of every integration.

  • camel get health takes a name or pid, like the other listings.

  • camel get endpoint, consumer and producer --limit keep the first rows after sorting (they kept the first rows found), and camel get processor --limit counts routes, as its description says.

  • camel dirty no longer keeps the files of another pid that starts with the same digits (pid 12 kept 123-status.json), nor removes the files of a process that is alive.

The JSON of the listings (--json) changes, so that a script can use the values without parsing text:

  • pid and the counters are numbers instead of text: total, failed, inflight, mean, min, max, last, delta and throughput. A value that is not there is null, instead of an empty or - text.

  • Values that combined several numbers are split: sinceLast (1s/2s/-) into sinceLastStarted, sinceLastCompleted and sinceLastFailed; the route of camel get context (3/4) into routesStarted and routesTotal; its reload into reloaded and reloadFailed; and in camel top context the load into load01, load05 and load15, heap into heapUsed, heapCommitted and heapMax (bytes), nonHeap into nonHeapUsed and nonHeapCommitted, gc into gcCount and gcTime (millis), and threads into threads and peakThreads. In camel get health, rl becomes readiness and liveness, rate becomes total, success and failure, and since becomes sinceLast, sinceStartSuccess and sinceStartFailure.

  • ready is a boolean (camel ps, camel get context), and camel ps has failed instead of fail.

  • Next to age (such as 5m3s), the listings have uptime in millis. Durations are millis: duration and elapsed of camel get blocked, inflight and internal-task, and time of camel get groovy, which lists its classes as an array.

  • The id of a route is routeId (it was route in camel get blocked, circuit-breaker, inflight and kafka), and the state is status (it was state in camel get circuit-breaker). camel get consumer has total instead of poll. Every row of camel get processor has the name of its integration.

camel-jbang - camel get route, processor, metric and health

  • camel get route --error --limit shows that number of routes with an error (it limited the routes first, and then showed the ones with an error among them), and --note shows the notes of the routes with an error too.

  • camel get processor --filter shows the matching processors with their route, and so with the name and pid of their integration (it showed the processors alone). --filter-mean leaves out the faster processors too, as its description says (it left out the faster routes only).

  • camel get metric shows the distribution summaries: an integration that has one was skipped with a warning. --filter takes a pattern such as timer*, as in its help (it showed nothing), and --custom leaves out the metrics of Camel in the default naming of Micrometer, such as camel.exchanges.total, not only in the classic naming (CamelExchangesTotal).

  • camel get health --level other than full or default exits with 2 (it showed every check, as full does).

camel-jbang - camel stop and camel restart wait for the integration

  • camel stop waits until the integration has stopped, up to --timeout (60 seconds by default), and exits with 124 when it has not stopped in time (use --kill to stop it at once). Before, it asked the integration to stop and exited with 0 at once; --no-wait does that. An integration without a pid file in ~/.camel is stopped with SIGTERM, which did nothing before.

  • camel stop and camel restart with a name or pid that matches no running integration exit with 3.

  • camel restart waits until the integration has stopped (terminating it when it does not stop within --timeout), and does not start it again while the old process is alive (exit 124). It waits until the new integration has started, and keeps its output in a log file in ~/.camel, which it prints (the log was removed when the command ended). A new integration that stops right away prints the end of its log and exits with 1; one that is still starting after --timeout exits with 124 and is left running.

camel-jbang - camel cmd browse of an endpoint that cannot be browsed

camel cmd browse --endpoint with an endpoint that the integration cannot browse, or that it does not have, exits with 3 (it printed an empty table and exited with 0).

camel-jbang - no command waits for a terminal that is not there

Without an interactive terminal, such as in a script, a CI job (the CI environment variable is set) or an AI agent:

  • --watch without --duration shows the output once, with a WARN: on stderr, instead of repeating until Enter is pressed. The new --duration option (such as --duration=30s) keeps watching for that long, and then exits with 0.

  • camel log, camel trace and camel cmd receive do not follow, unless --follow or --duration is given. camel infra log prints the end of the logs and exits, and has --follow and --duration too.

  • camel hawtio does not open a web browser, and prints the URL of the web console instead. --openUrl is now named --open-url (the old name still works).

  • camel debug, camel nano, camel shell, camel edit, camel tui (except with --record), camel ask without a question, camel run --prompt and camel get history --it exit with 2 and requires an interactive terminal on stderr. camel debug used to wait at 100% CPU. camel debug --background does not require an interactive terminal.

--follow and --open-url without a value turn the option on. As boolean options that are on by default, they turned it off (use --follow=false to turn it off). In a terminal, the end of the input (Ctrl+D) stops a command that watches or follows, like Enter does.

camel-jbang - camel cmd route-diagram and camel cmd span

  • camel cmd route-diagram --highlight with node ids that no route has exits with 3 (it drew the diagram with nothing highlighted). Its errors are printed on stderr, and a graphical --theme in a terminal without graphics exits with 2.

  • camel cmd span --trace with a trace id that matches no trace exits with 3 (it printed a message and exited with 0), and an unknown --sort key of the traces exits with 2 (it sorted the newest first).

camel-jbang - camel run: startup in the background, and input errors

  • camel run --background waits up to 60 seconds for the integration to start (it was 20 seconds), and --background-wait takes how long, such as --background-wait=2m (true and false still work). An integration that is still starting after the wait exits with 124 and is left running; before, the command exited with 0.

  • The output of an integration in the background goes to ~/.camel/<pid>-run.log, whose path is printed. The log is kept while the integration runs (it was removed once the integration had started), and camel stop removes it. With --background-wait=false the output goes to the log as well, instead of a pipe that nobody read.

  • An integration that loads no routes exits with 1 and No routes found in: <files>, instead of running without doing anything, except with --source-dir, --empty or in dev mode, where files can be added later.

  • camel run and camel dev with --max-seconds, --max-messages or --max-idle-seconds exit at the limit also in dev mode, which stopped the routes and kept running. A limit from the configuration (camel.main.durationMax…​) still only stops the routes in dev mode.

  • A file that does not exist exits with 66 and File does not exist: <file> for every kind of file, before anything is printed. Files together with --source-dir, and --background with a runtime that does not support it, exit with 2.

  • Only a run of nothing but Citrus tests exits with 2 and says to run them with camel test run, before anything is printed: camel run test/orders.citrus.it.yaml, or camel run test/ of a folder that holds only Citrus tests. Before, it ran an integration with no routes. Citrus tests given with routes, such as by camel run *, are skipped with a line each (Skipping <file>: a Citrus test, not a route (run it with camel test run <file>)), and the routes run. A directory or --source-dir with a test folder runs as before. The camel_run tool of the AI tools does the same: it starts nothing for Citrus tests only, and its answer lists the tests it skipped.

camel-jbang - camel explain and camel harden

  • The LLM configuration, the progress and the prompts of --show-prompt are printed on stderr, so stdout has only the answer of the LLM (with a header per file), such as for camel explain route.yaml > explanation.md.

  • An unknown --format exits with 2 (it was ignored).

  • camel harden --api-type=openai prints the suggestions (it printed only the header): the OpenAI-compatible service answers in one go, also with --stream.

  • An API key of eight characters or fewer is not shown in the configuration (the last four characters of a key were).

camel-jbang - the AI tools report failures as errors

  • camel ask runs a camel command for the model with a timeout of 60 seconds, and gives it the exit code, stdout and stderr as JSON (it gave the output only, and the exit code only when there was no output). A command that runs until it is stopped is not run, and the model is told the form that returns, such as camel run --background.

  • The MCP tools of camel mcp and camel tui --mcp answer a failure with isError, instead of a normal answer that says what went wrong: camel_run of an integration that stops while it starts (status is failed), a restart that fails (camel_control), an action the integration answers with an error or does not answer (such as stopping a route that does not exist), and camel_runtime_ai_trace when the integration does not answer. The text of the error has what the answer had, such as the output of the integration.

  • camel_control restart in camel tui --mcp waits until the integration has started again, or failed to start (it answered at once), and camel_control stop-routes and start-routes answer with what was done instead of the answer of the integration.

camel-jbang - the errors of a streamed answer of an LLM

  • When the streamed answer of an LLM fails, as with camel explain, camel ask or camel overview --ai against Ollama, the error is printed on stderr: it was printed on stdout after the answer so far. A failure without a message of its own, such as a refused connection, has the message of its cause instead of null.

  • camel ask with an OpenAI-compatible server that gives the arguments of a tool call as a JSON object, instead of JSON text, calls the tool with these arguments (it called it without arguments).

camel-jbang - camel ask: the exit code and the output of a question

  • camel ask exits with 1 when the call of the LLM fails, such as with an error of the model or a refused connection: it exited with 0 without an answer.

  • camel ask with a question prints only the answer on stdout. The model it uses, the integration it asks about, the note about a small model, and the output of --show-tools and --show-stats go to stderr.

camel-jbang - an LLM given with --url is the only one used

camel ask, camel explain and camel overview --ai fail when the LLM given with --url does not answer: they went on to another provider, such as the Ollama of the host, or a hosted provider with an API key in the environment, and sent the prompts there. The AI panel of the Camel TUI does the same with the URL of its settings. Without --url the provider is detected as before.

camel-jbang - exit codes and errors of more commands

  • camel export does not clean the export directory when it is the current directory, or one that contains it, unless --clean-dir is given: --dir=./, --dir=$PWD and --dir=.. deleted the files of the project, as only --dir=. was recognized.

  • camel harden --url with an LLM that cannot be reached exits with 1, instead of sending the route to a local Ollama.

  • camel sbom with an unknown --sbom-format or --sbom-output-format exits with 2 (it exited with 0 without doing anything), and exits with 124 when Maven does not finish in time.

  • camel update run --upgradeTimeout applies (the output was read to the end before the wait), and exits with 124 when it is reached.

  • camel version list exits with 1 when the versions cannot be downloaded. camel version set --reset removes the Kamelets version too, and camel version get shows a configuration with only the Kamelets version.

  • camel validate yaml and camel validate properties with no file to validate exit with 66.

  • camel doc looks a name up in the catalog of Camel before it downloads the Kamelet catalog, so a component is documented also offline. camel catalog kamelet has --repos.

  • camel kubernetes logs exits with 3 when no pod with the label shows up, and with 1 when reading the log fails. camel kubernetes run prints the end of the Maven output when the build or deployment fails (it was hidden without --verbose), and exits with 124 when the pod is not running after 10 minutes.

  • --jvm-args keeps a quoted value with spaces as one argument.

camel-jbang - processes started by the CLI stop with it

  • When camel run of a Spring Boot or Quarkus project (and camel debug) ends, such as on Ctrl+C, the processes started by Maven stop too: the app could outlive Maven, as it runs in a JVM of its own. A process that does not stop within 30 seconds is killed (it was left running).

  • A plugin that cannot be loaded ends the command with exit code 1 and the error, as before, but no longer exits the JVM while the plugins load, which ended a program that embeds the CLI.

camel-jbang - camel overview and camel transform route

  • camel overview --format other than markdown or json exits with 2 (it printed the markdown).

  • camel transform route --format other than xml, yaml or java exits with 2 (it ran the routes and printed nothing), and a route that cannot be read, such as invalid YAML, exits with 1 and the error of the parser (it failed with an internal error, 70, and a stack trace).

camel-xml-io, camel-yaml-io, camel-java-io - route dumps keep options set to their default value

The XML, YAML and Java route dumps (camel transform route, camel validate normalize, camel.main.dumpRoutes, the developer console) now write these options whenever they are set, also with their documented default value: the binding options of the rest configuration, a rest and its verbs (such as bindingMode="off"), the options of a redelivery policy, of a resilience4jConfiguration and of a faultToleranceConfiguration, shutdownRoute / shutdownRunningTask of a route, and apiDocs of a rest verb. Left out, such an option took the value of the parent level when the dump was loaded (the rest configuration or the rest, the error handler, the global circuit breaker configuration, the CamelContext), so the route behaved differently, or, for apiDocs, a verb with apiDocs="true" under a rest with apiDocs="false" was left out of the OpenAPI document.

camel-yaml-dsl - a value under parameters: with an & is kept whole

In the YAML DSL, a value under parameters: that has an & is now kept whole in the endpoint uri. Before, the value was cut at the &, and the rest was read as another option. For example, with filterFile: "${file:name} contains 'R&D'" the route failed to start. A lenient component, such as a Kamelet, got only the text before the &, without an error.

Camel now puts such a value in RAW(…​) in the uri, as it does for a secret option. When the value has )&, Camel uses RAW{…​}. The value is then taken as it is written, so a ` in it stays a `. A value without an & is read as before, so a ` in it is a space (such as in a cron expression written with `).

This applies to the endpoints of from, to, toD, wireTap, poll and switch, and to the kamelet EIP. Camel does not change a value that starts with # (a bean reference), a value in RAW(…​) or RAW{…​} already, or a path parameter, such as directoryName when the uri is only file: an & does no harm in the path. A route dump, such as camel validate normalize, writes such a value in RAW.

Camel sees the & only when it is written in the YAML as the value of an option. When the & comes from a property placeholder, or is in a value of a map option (such as additionalProperties), write the value in RAW yourself, such as RAW({{name}}).

camel-jbang - camel get bean, get route-controller and top source

  • camel get bean --dsl shows the configuration of each property next to that property: it paired them by position, which showed the configuration of another property, or failed with an internal error (70) with --nulls=false. An unknown --scope exits with 2 (it showed every bean).

  • camel get route-controller shows back-off times and attempts that do not fit in an int (it showed other numbers), and no longer shows Starting Routes: false for the default route controller, which does not tell.

  • camel top source --json has the counters total, mean, min, max and last as numbers instead of text, as the other listings.

camel-jbang - a boolean option given without a value is on

A boolean option that is on by default is now on when it is given without a value, like any other boolean option: camel export --maven-wrapper exports the Maven wrapper, camel config set --global key=value writes the global configuration, camel cmd receive --show-body shows the body, and camel kubernetes run --dev --cleanup removes the deployment when it stops. Such a flag turned the option off before (picocli sets a flag to the opposite of its default). Turn the option off with =false, such as --maven-wrapper=false, which works as before.

camel-jbang - an unknown sort key is a usage error

The commands that sort their rows by --sort, such as camel ps, camel get route, camel cmd thread-dump or camel catalog component, exit with 2 and list their keys when the key is unknown: camel ps --sort=nmae printed the integrations unsorted. A row without the value of the key now comes first, instead of failing the command.

camel-jbang - camel catalog kamelet

camel catalog kamelet --filter no longer fails with an internal error (70) when a Kamelet has no description. An unknown --type exits with 2 (it showed nothing), a --filter that matches nothing says so on stderr, and --json prints the Kamelets as JSON, as the other camel catalog commands do.

camel-jbang - camel init does not overwrite a file

camel init exits with 1 when the file it creates exists already, and leaves the file as it is (it overwrote the file before). Use --force to overwrite it. The same holds for the files camel init downloads from GitHub or a gist, of which it writes none when one of them exists. When the template is picked in the terminal (camel init without a file), the command asks there whether to overwrite the file.

camel-jbang - camel get history

  • camel get history --diagram and --it exit with 2 when several integrations have a message history, and ask for the name or pid of one (they printed a message on stdout and exited with 1). The errors of --diagram are printed on stderr, and a graphical --theme in a terminal without graphics exits with 2.

  • camel get history --json --mask masks the secrets of the endpoints in the JSON too, as in the table.

camel-jbang - exit codes of config set, dependency list, jolokia and cmd memory-leak

  • camel jolokia with a name or pid that matches no running integration, or more than one, exits with 3, as camel hawtio does (it exited with 1, and printed the second case on stdout).

  • camel config set without a key (=value stored a value with an empty key) or without a value exits with 2, and prints the error on stderr.

  • camel dependency list with an unknown --output exits with 2.

  • camel cmd memory-leak --min-size takes a size in bytes such as 10B, and a value that is not a size exits with 2 (both turned the filter off).

  • camel cmd send --reply to a route that fails with an exception without a message, such as a NullPointerException, prints the failed exchange and exits with 1, instead of failing with a ClassCastException.

  • camel get source of an integration with routes without a source location, such as routes added by a RouteBuilder, no longer fails.

  • Versions with four parts, such as the Quarkus platform version 3.27.3.1, compare as versions in the version checks of the CLI (3.27.3.1 came after 3.27.4), and a -SNAPSHOT always comes before its release, as in Maven (4.24.10-SNAPSHOT came after 4.24.10).

camel-jbang - camel dependency copy, update run and get error

  • camel dependency copy deletes the JAR files an earlier copy left in --output-directory, and keeps its other files: it deleted the whole directory (with --output-directory=. the project). It exits with 1 when a dependency cannot be resolved or copied (it printed the error and exited with 0), and with 2 when --output-directory is a file.

  • camel update run --runtime=quarkus --download=false without --quarkusMavenPluginVersion exits with 2, and a version without upgrade recipes exits with 1: both failed with an internal error (70).

  • camel get error --last shows the newest error of all the integrations (it showed the newest error of the integration with the lowest pid). An --ago that is not a duration, and a --handled that is not true or false, exit with 2: the first showed every error, the second none. The errors of --diagram are printed on stderr, and a graphical --theme in a terminal without graphics exits with 2.

camel-jbang - camel version list

  • camel version list prints the Last updated note on stderr, so stdout has the table only.

  • A --date-format that is not a date pattern, and a --from-date or --to-date that is not a date in the yyyy-MM-dd format (such as 2025-01-31), exit with 2: the first showed the dates unformatted, and the others were ignored.

camel-jbang - the shared action file is deprecated for plugin commands

CamelCommand.getActionFile and CamelCommand.getOutputFile, the action and output files that all the clients of an integration share, are deprecated. A plugin command sends an action with ActionClient instead, which gives each action a file of its own, so that two clients cannot overwrite each other’s action or answer, and uses the shared files only for an integration before Camel 4.21. camel debug sends its commands this way too.

camel-jbang - camel log, trace, cmd receive, cmd send, transform message and cmd memory-leak

  • camel log, camel trace --action=dump and camel cmd receive --action=dump exit with 3 when the name or pid matches no running integration, and they do not follow (in a script or an AI agent, or with --follow=false): they printed nothing and exited with 0.

  • camel cmd receive --action=dump without a name shows the messages of the running integrations (it showed nothing). With --output=json it prints each received message once, as one JSON document per line with its exchangeId: it printed the batch of messages a line of the received file holds once for each message in it. --since, --grep and --mask apply to the JSON too.

  • camel trace and camel cmd receive exit with 2 for an unknown --sort key, as the other listings do.

  • camel trace --tail shows the end of the last exchange as completed, not as returning from its route.

  • camel cmd send and camel transform message print the error and exit with 1 when the integration fails the action itself (they failed with an internal error, 70).

  • camel cmd memory-leak exits with 1 when the integration fails the action, such as when it cannot record, or has no jfr-memory-leak dev console: it printed nothing and exited with 0. --status shows the progress of a recording (it said the recording started), and after a recording that its results can be queried (it said that no samples were captured).

camel-jbang - camel cmd eval with quotes and slashes

camel cmd eval, and the tools that evaluate an expression in a running integration (camel ask, and camel_eval_expression of camel mcp and camel tui --mcp with an integration selected), send the expression and the body as they are. They escaped them, which the integration does not undo: an expression with a double quote failed, such as ${body} == "hello", and a slash (as in every XPath) or a quote in the body became part of the text.

camel-jbang - the runtime tools of camel ask and the MCP servers

  • send_message of camel ask, and tui_send_message of camel tui --mcp, send the headers given as key=value lines, as their description says: the integration got the lines as one text, which it could not read as headers, and failed the message. A line that is not key=value is an error of the tool.

  • get_circuit_breakers has the calls of the fallback, the calls that timed out and the calls a full bulkhead rejected, as camel get circuit-breaker shows them.

  • get_route_analysis, get_eip_stats and detect_config_drift have the answers of the integration as JSON, instead of as JSON text inside their JSON.

camel-jbang-mcp - migration and dependency tools

The camel_migration_compatibility tool no longer takes the camelComponents argument, which it never used. It now takes the Java requirement from the JDK versions of the target release in the catalog (the same data as camel_version_list): Camel 4.19 and later no longer report Java 17 as a blocker, and javaCompatibility also lists the supportedVersions. A target version newer than every release in the catalog has no Java requirement: the tool says so in warnings instead of taking the one of the newest release.

camel_migration_recipes returns commands that run: the Spring Boot command activates org.apache.camel.upgrade.CamelSpringBootMigrationRecipe, the recipes artifact is the <major>.<minor>.0 release of the target version, the OpenRewrite plugin is no longer pinned to a version, and the Quarkus command runs the quarkus-maven-plugin at the Quarkus platform version of the new optional quarkusPlatformVersion argument (or the latest one), with -Drewrite=true for a full run.

In camel_dependency_check and camel_dependency_security_audit the dependencies are the declared ones: a component only in dependencyManagement or in the dependencies of a plugin no longer counts, and Spring Boot starters and Camel Quarkus extensions count as the component they provide. The version status of camel_dependency_check compares the project with the latest Camel release, added as latestVersion, instead of the catalog version of the server. The security audit no longer audits the server’s catalog version when the pom.xml sets no Camel version: it audits the camelVersion argument, else lists every advisory of the declared components, and says so in warnings. Its reachable is null when no routes are given, advisories without components are returned as uncheckedAdvisories, and clean is true only when none of them apply.

camel-jbang-mcp - camel_openapi_mock_guidance is removed

The camel_openapi_mock_guidance MCP tool is removed. Call camel_openapi_scaffold with missingOperation=mock instead: its answer explains the mode and lists the camel-mock/ files, one per path of the spec. The scaffold and camel_openapi_validate now follow the rules of camel-rest-openapi: an operation without operationId is routed to direct:GENOPID_<METHOD><path> with / replaced by . and the braces of path parameters by (direct:GENOPID_GET.pets._petId for GET /pets/{petId}), and a mock file has the request path as its name, without the HTTP method (camel-mock/pets/42.json for GET /pets/42). The security scheme diagnostics of camel_openapi_validate are now all warnings with a SECURITY_<type> code (SECURITY_APIKEY, SECURITY_HTTP, …​), as camel-rest-openapi enforces none of them, and OPENAPI_31 is reported as information instead of a warning.

camel-jpa, camel-hazelcast - an exchange marked rollback only is not consumed

When a route marks the exchange rollback only (for example with markRollbackOnly() or markRollbackOnlyLast()) there is no exception on the exchange, and these consumers treated it as a success. The JPA consumer deleted the entity (or called its @Consumed method) and committed the poll, and the transacted (transacted=true) Hazelcast SEDA consumer committed its transaction, so the message was removed from the queue. Now a rollback only exchange is not consumed: the JPA consumer leaves the entity in the table, so the next poll consumes it again, as long as it still matches the query. With transacted=false the other entities of the poll are still consumed (without logging a WARN), and so is what a @PreConsumed method changed before routing; with transacted=true the whole poll is rolled back, as for a failed exchange. The Hazelcast SEDA consumer rolls back its transaction, so the message stays in the queue.

A message that the route marks rollback only every time was removed before, and is now consumed again at every poll (JPA), or taken from the queue again at once, without a delay (transacted Hazelcast SEDA, as for a failed exchange). Handle such a message in the route instead, for example by moving it aside, rather than marking it rollback only every time.

camel-jpa - a consumer query that selects values delivers them

A JPA consumer whose query selects values that are not entities, such as a column (select x.address from Customer x), now delivers them with the default consumeLockEntity=true. Before, the lock of each value failed and the value was skipped, so the route received no message, with only a DEBUG log. Such a value cannot be deleted or marked with @Consumed, so each poll delivers the same values again, whatever consumeDelete is: a route that received nothing now gets the whole result on each poll. Make the query exclude the rows already handled, or select the entity instead. With the default consumeDelete=true a WARN is logged once; set consumeDelete=false to avoid it.

camel-a2a - caller profile and task owner

  • When a security requirement of the agent card combines several schemes, such as an API key and an OAuth token, the caller profile (CamelA2AUserProfile) is now the one of the token, whatever the order of the schemes. Before, it was the profile of the first scheme, so a requirement that listed the API key first only gave {"scheme": "apiKey"} to the route, and the tasks belonged to the API key.

  • A token without a sub claim is now identified by the username that a token introspection response gives, else by its client_id or azp claim (such as a client credentials token), so the tasks of different users and clients are kept apart. A valid token that has none of these claims is now rejected with HTTP 401.

  • The CamelOAuthTokenValidationResult exchange property is now set on the exchange that the route processes, as documented. Before, it was only set on the incoming HTTP exchange.

camel-hazelcast - the transacted SEDA consumer waits for an exchange that continues on another thread

A Hazelcast transaction can only be committed or rolled back by the thread that began it. When the route of a transacted (transacted=true) Hazelcast SEDA consumer continued on another thread (an asynchronous producer, or threads()), the consumer tried to commit or roll back the transaction on that thread, which failed with Transaction cannot span multiple threads! (logged as a WARN and ignored). The message was neither removed nor put back: a failed message was not taken again, and the messages, also the processed ones, were put back into the queue when the application left the Hazelcast cluster, so they were consumed again. Now the consumer thread waits for the exchange to complete and ends the transaction itself, so it takes the next message only then; use concurrentConsumers to process several messages at the same time. The exchange must complete within the Hazelcast transaction timeout (2 minutes by default, TransactionOptions): when it takes longer, the commit fails with TransactionTimedOutException, which is logged at ERROR, the transaction is rolled back and the message is consumed again, also when the route succeeded.

camel-hazelcast - the reliable topic consumer goes on after it lost messages

A hazelcast-topic consumer with reliable=true that fell behind the ringbuffer of the reliable topic (messages overwritten, or expired by its time to live, before the consumer read them) stopped receiving messages for good, while the route stayed started: Hazelcast removed its listener. The consumer now skips the lost messages, logs a WARN with their number, and goes on with the oldest message still in the ringbuffer. When Hazelcast cancels the listener for another reason, such as a destroyed topic, the consumer reports it to its exception handler (logged at WARN by default); restart the route to receive messages again. A listener cancelled because the Hazelcast instance or the CamelContext is shutting down is only logged at DEBUG.

camel-sql, camel-mybatis, camel-jooq - an exchange marked rollback only last is not consumed either

The SQL, MyBatis and jOOQ consumers already left a message unconsumed when the route marked the exchange rollback only with markRollbackOnly(). They now leave it unconsumed when the route marks it rollback only last with markRollbackOnlyLast() too, as the JPA and Hazelcast SEDA consumers do: the SQL consumer runs its onConsumeFailed statement instead of onConsume (and no statement when onConsumeFailed is not set), the MyBatis consumer does not run its onConsume statement, and the jOOQ consumer does not delete the entity, so the next poll reads the row again. With transacted=true the SQL and MyBatis consumers break out of the batch and roll back. markRollbackOnlyLast() reaches the consumer only when no transacted() EIP has consumed the flag, so the consumer’s own transaction is the last one.

camel-sql - sql and sql-stored with batch=true: the update count and a single Map body

With batch=true the CamelSqlUpdateCount (sql) and CamelSqlStoredUpdateCount (sql-stored) headers no longer add the negative values a JDBC driver returns for an item whose update count it does not know (Statement.SUCCESS_NO_INFO, which HSQLDB returns for stored procedure calls). Such a batch reported a negative count before, for example -4 for two items; it now reports the sum of the known counts, which can be 0.

A single Map body is now one batch item, and so is a single Map in the CamelSqlParameters header with useMessageBodyForSql=true (sql) or the CamelSqlStoredParameters header with useMessageBodyForTemplate=true (sql-stored). Before, its entries were used as the batch items and the call failed (sql: no value found for the named parameters, sql-stored: an SQLException).

camel-sql - JdbcKeyValueRepository: deleteIfExpiredString, and putIfAbsent after a duplicate key

When JdbcKeyValueRepository reads an entry that has expired, it deletes the row with the new deleteIfExpiredString statement (DELETE FROM CAMEL_KEYVALUE WHERE ITEM_KEY = ? AND EXPIRES_AT > 0 AND EXPIRES_AT ⇐ ?) instead of deleteString, so that an entry another node stored for the key in the meantime is kept. If you set selectString, insertString and deleteString for a table with other column names, set deleteIfExpiredString as well; otherwise reading an expired entry fails with an SQL error. With tableName it is derived from the default statement, as the other statements are.

When the INSERT of putIfAbsent fails with a duplicate key, the key is read again in a new transaction, which returns the value stored by the other caller (before, the key was read again in the failed transaction, which could return null although nothing was stored). putIfAbsent now throws the DuplicateKeyException when the INSERT fails three times, or at once when it runs in a transaction of the caller, as that transaction cannot be used to read the key again.

camel-sql - JdbcKeyValueRepository: replace and delete(key, expected) do not match an expired entry

JdbcKeyValueRepository.replace(key, expected, newValue, ttl) and delete(key, expected) now match only an entry that has not expired. Before, they matched an entry whose TTL had passed and returned true, although get returns null for it, and replace gave that entry a new TTL. Now they return false and leave the row as it is.

For this, the default updateIfValueString and deleteIfValueString statements end with AND (EXPIRES_AT = 0 OR EXPIRES_AT > ?), the condition of selectKeysString, and the current time is passed as their last parameter:

UPDATE CAMEL_KEYVALUE SET ITEM_VALUE = ?, EXPIRES_AT = ?
    WHERE ITEM_KEY = ? AND ITEM_VALUE = ? AND (EXPIRES_AT = 0 OR EXPIRES_AT > ?)
DELETE FROM CAMEL_KEYVALUE WHERE ITEM_KEY = ? AND ITEM_VALUE = ? AND (EXPIRES_AT = 0 OR EXPIRES_AT > ?)

If you set updateIfValueString or deleteIfValueString yourself, for example for a table with other column names, add the same condition with a parameter for the current time at the end. Otherwise the repository passes one more parameter than the statement has placeholders: most JDBC drivers then fail with an SQL error, while some drivers may ignore the extra parameter, so the statement still matches an expired entry. With tableName the statements are derived from the default statements, as before.

camel-jta - an exchange marked rollback only last rolls back its transaction

In a transacted() route that uses a JTA transaction manager, markRollbackOnlyLast() now rolls back the transaction of that transacted() block, as it already did with the camel-spring transaction error handler. Before, the transaction was committed, and the marker was then cleared, so nothing showed that the rollback was skipped. The marker is still cleared afterwards, so an outer transacted() block does not see it.

If the transacted() block in which the exchange is marked rollback only last joins the transaction of an outer block (for example a PROPAGATION_MANDATORY block inside a PROPAGATION_REQUIRED block), that shared JTA transaction is now marked rollback only, so the commit of the outer block fails (in Camel Quarkus with CamelException: Unable to commit transaction) where it used to commit, as with camel-spring (UnexpectedRollbackException). To roll back only the inner block, use PROPAGATION_REQUIRES_NEW for it.

camel-keycloak - the CamelKeycloakOperation header needs the allowOperationHeader option

The CamelKeycloakOperation header took the place of the operation of the keycloak endpoint, so a message could make the endpoint run another operation than the one it is configured with. The header is now ignored unless the endpoint sets the new allowOperationHeader=true option (disabled by default), and the endpoint runs its configured operation. A route that picks the operation with the header must set allowOperationHeader=true on the endpoint, or on the component (camel.component.keycloak.allowOperationHeader=true) for all its endpoints.

An endpoint without an operation, which took the operation from the header, now fails with an IllegalArgumentException that asks for the operation, unless it sets allowOperationHeader=true.

camel-thrift - SSL/TLS settings of the consumer, and errors sent to the client

  • A consumer configured with negotiationType=SSL and the default synchronous=false now fails to start. It used to start a server without SSL/TLS. SSL/TLS requires synchronous=true, as on the producer.

  • The SASL negotiation type, which is not implemented, now makes the producer and the consumer fail to start. They used to behave as with PLAINTEXT.

  • The SSL/TLS consumer now creates its server socket from the SSL context of sslParameters, so all of its settings apply, including the trust managers and the server parameters. For example, serverParameters.clientAuthentication=REQUIRE now requires a client certificate. Before, the consumer only used the key store, the protocol and the cipher suites, and never asked for a client certificate. The key managers are required, and as in the other components the key is read with their keyPassword: set it when the key is protected by a password. Before, the consumer read the key with the password of the key store.

  • A new muteException consumer option, enabled by default, sends a generic TApplicationException of type INTERNAL_ERROR ("Internal error processing <method>") to the client instead of an exception that the Thrift method does not declare. Before, the asynchronous consumer sent the message of the exception, and the synchronous consumer sent a TApplicationException as it was, such as one returned by another Thrift service that the route called. An exception declared in the IDL is still sent as it is. Set muteException=false to send the exception as before.

camel-mina - sync=false is one-way, and the reply of a textline consumer

A consumer with sync=false no longer writes the body the route ends with back to the client. Before, it wrote it back (over TCP and UDP), so a client that only sends got its own message echoed. Use sync=true (the default) when the client expects a reply.

A producer with sync=false now leaves the body and headers of an InOut exchange as they were. Before, the exchange ended with an empty message.

A consumer with textline=true now converts the reply to a String before writing it, as the producer does with the request. Before, a byte[] or a stream reply was written as its toString().

A consumer with disconnectOnNoReply=false now keeps the session open when the route ends with no body, and logs it at noReplyLogLevel (WARN by default). Before, the consumer always closed the session and logged at DEBUG.

camel-thrift - the reply of the consumer must convert to the return type of the method

For a method with a return type, the consumer now fails the call with a TApplicationException when the route leaves no body (type MISSING_RESULT), or a body that does not convert to the return type (type INTERNAL_ERROR). Before, the synchronous consumer sent 0 (or false) for a method that returns a number (or a bool) and the route left no body, and the asynchronous consumer sent a null result, or no response at all for a method that returns a number. The body is still ignored for a void method.

camel-netty - errors sent to the client

A new muteException consumer option, enabled by default, makes a consumer with sync=true write a generic exception (java.lang.Exception with the message Exchange processing failed and no stack trace) back to the client when the exchange failed, instead of the exception of the route. With textline=true the client now receives the line java.lang.Exception: Exchange processing failed, where it used to receive the class name and the message of the exception of the route. With an encoder that accepts any object, the serialised exception no longer carries the class, message, cause or stack trace of the exception of the route. Set muteException=false to write the exception as before. transferExchange=true is not affected.

camel-netty - replies with the default codec, useByteBuf and sync=false

With the default codec (no textline, no custom encoders or decoders) a consumer with sync=true now converts a reply that is not a String (or a ByteBuf) to a String before writing it, as with textline=true. Before, such a reply, including the exception of a failed exchange, could not be written and the client got no reply. A failed exchange now replies java.lang.Exception: Exchange processing failed (see muteException above). When the reply cannot be written, the consumer closes the TCP channel instead of leaving the client waiting.

With useByteBuf=true a body that cannot be converted to a ByteBuf (such as a File, a Map or a POJO) now fails the exchange with an InvalidPayloadException, where nothing was sent before. A consumer with useByteBuf=true now also converts a reply set as the out message to a ByteBuf. NettyPayloadHelper.getIn and getOut declare InvalidPayloadException.

With sync=false the producer now ignores a message the remote server sends back. Before, that reply was set on the exchange after it had already continued routing. The producer now also leaves the body and headers of an InOut exchange as they were. Before, the exchange continued with an empty message. This also applies to netty-http.

camel-netty - a reply that cannot be written, and allowSerializedHeaders on the reply

When writing the reply fails (for example the encoder cannot encode it), the consumer now closes the channel (not for UDP), so the client does not wait for a reply that never comes. Before, the failure was only logged.

With transferExchange=true and allowSerializedHeaders=true, the consumer now also includes serializable header, property and variable values in a reply set as the out message, as it already did for any other reply. The decoder of the other side must accept their classes.

With transferExchange=true, the producer and the consumer no longer transfer the headers and exchange properties that hold Netty transport objects of their own side: CamelNettyRemoteAddress, CamelNettyLocalAddress, CamelNettySSLSession, CamelNettyChannelHandlerContext, CamelNettyMessageEvent and CamelNettyChannel. Before, with allowSerializedHeaders=true, the socket address headers (java.net.InetSocketAddress) were transferred, and the default deserialization filter of ObjectDecoder, which rejects java.net classes, made the other side fail to decode the message unless the route removed them.

camel-netty - with transferExchange the consumer keeps its own connection headers

With transferExchange=true the connection headers CamelNettyRemoteAddress, CamelNettyLocalAddress, CamelNettyChannelHandlerContext, CamelNettySSLSession and the CamelNettySSLClientCert* headers (and the CamelNettyRemoteAddress and CamelNettyChannel exchange properties) are now always the ones of the receiving side: the connection it accepted is the source of truth. Before, the headers of the transferred exchange replaced them. Such a header in the transferred exchange is no longer used, also when the receiving side has no value for it (for example the CamelNettySSLClientCert* headers on a connection without a client certificate). This also applies to the reply the producer receives. Send any such value of the sender under a header name of your own.

camel-netty - transferExchange requires explicit endpoint configuration

NettyPayloadHelper now checks transferExchange=true on the endpoint configuration before reconstructing exchange data from a DefaultExchangeHolder payload.

Before, a receiver with an object decoder silently reconstructed the exchange even when transferExchange=false. Now, when transferExchange is disabled (the default), the incoming DefaultExchangeHolder is left as an opaque body (both on the consumer side and for the producer’s reply via setOut).

In addition, the legacy two-argument methods NettyPayloadHelper.setIn(Exchange, Object) and setOut(Exchange, Object) are deprecated and no longer unmarshal DefaultExchangeHolder payloads. Callers should migrate to the three-argument overloads passing the NettyEndpoint.

camel-netty-http - a response that cannot be built

When no HTTP response can be built from the reply of the route (for example the body cannot be converted), the consumer now answers with HTTP Status 500 and closes the connection. The body is empty, or the stack trace of the exception with muteException=false. Before, no response was sent and the client waited until it timed out.

A List reply is now written as its toString(), as a Map or a POJO already was. Before, no response was sent.

camel-odata - the body of CREATE and UPDATE, and a response that is not a JSON object

CREATE and UPDATE send JSON text given as a byte array, a stream or a file as it is, as they do for a String. Before, a byte array was sent as a JSON array of numbers, and a stream or a file failed.

CREATE and UPDATE now fail with an IllegalArgumentException when the body is null or empty. Before, they sent a POST or PATCH request with no content. Set the body to {} to send an empty JSON object.

A response that is a JSON array or a plain value is now the body as it is parsed (a List, a number, a String or a boolean). Before, the producer failed with Expected an OData JSON object response.

camel-telegram - webhook requests are verified with a secret token

In webhook mode, Camel now registers the webhook with a secret token, which Telegram sends in the X-Telegram-Bot-Api-Secret-Token header of every webhook request, and rejects a webhook request that does not carry the token with HTTP 403. The token is the value of the new webhookSecretToken option or, when that option is not set, a value derived from the authorization token. A client that calls the webhook endpoint directly, for example in a test, must send the header too.

When Camel registers the webhook at startup (webhookAutoRegister=true, the default), nothing needs to change: each upgraded instance registers the webhook with the token, and the instances that are not upgraded yet ignore the header.

When the webhook is registered in another way, through the WebhookRoutePolicy or outside Camel, the registration made with the previous version has no secret token, and the upgraded instances reject every update until the webhook is registered again with it. Register the webhook again first, then upgrade the instances that receive the updates: instances of the previous version ignore the header. With the WebhookRoutePolicy, run the registration with the new version. Outside Camel, set webhookSecretToken, and pass the same value as the secret_token parameter of setWebhook.

The default webhook path is a hash of the endpoint URI, so adding an option to the URI, such as webhookSecretToken, changes the path. When the webhook is not registered by Camel at startup, set webhookPath to keep the path that is registered.

To restore the previous behaviour, set webhookSecretTokenEnabled=false: the webhook is then registered without a secret token, and webhook requests are not verified.

camel-whatsapp - webhook events are verified by default

In webhook mode (webhook:whatsapp:…​), the X-Hub-Signature-256 signature of every event that WhatsApp posts is now verified with the webhookSecret option, the app secret from the Meta app dashboard. A webhook route without webhookSecret now fails to start. Set webhookSecret to the app secret, or set the new webhookVerification option to false to accept the events without verifying their signature, for example in development. An event without a valid signature is answered with HTTP 403 and does not reach the route. A client that posts events to the webhook endpoint directly, for example in a test, must sign them too.

An event that reaches the route is now answered with HTTP 200 and an empty body. Before, the reply carried the event back to the caller. The reply to the verification request (GET) is still the hub.challenge.

camel-paho-mqtt5 - a failed message with manualAcksEnabled is delivered again or acknowledged

With manualAcksEnabled=true, a message whose exchange failed was left unacknowledged. For a QoS 1 message the broker never sent it again during the connection, and after receiveMaximum such messages it sent the consumer nothing more (the client completes the QoS 2 flow on its own, so QoS 2 messages did not stop the consumer). Now, for a QoS 1 message on a session that the broker keeps after the disconnect (cleanStart=false and sessionExpiryInterval greater than 0), the consumer connects again after a failed exchange (after one second, doubled for each failed exchange in a row up to maxReconnectDelay, and back to one second after any exchange that succeeds; logged at WARN), and the broker sends the message again. In all other cases (a clean session, which is the default, a QoS 2 message, or cleanStart=false without sessionExpiryInterval, where the session ends with the connection) the broker never sends the message again, so the failed message is acknowledged and the failure is left to the Camel error handler. A consumer that uses a client set with the client option leaves a failed QoS 1 message on a session that the broker keeps unacknowledged, as before; as the consumer does not connect that client, set the cleanStart and sessionExpiryInterval of the endpoint to match its connection options.

camel-iggy - messageExpiry is sent to Iggy in microseconds

The messageExpiry option is in seconds, but its value was passed unchanged to Iggy, which reads the message expiry of a topic in microseconds, so a topic created by Camel (autoCreateTopic=true) expired its messages a million times too early (messageExpiry=3600 gave 3.6 ms instead of one hour). The value is now converted to microseconds. Topics created before keep their expiry: change it on the Iggy server. If you set messageExpiry in microseconds to work around this, set it in seconds now, or newly created topics keep their messages a million times longer than intended.

camel-iggy - binary payloads, and the body of the consumer

The iggy producer now sends a byte[], InputStream, ByteBuffer or file body as its bytes. Previously it converted it to text first, which corrupted a payload that is not valid UTF-8. Other bodies are still sent as UTF-8 text. A null body now fails with an InvalidPayloadException, a List body with a null element fails with a clear message, and an empty List body sends nothing (all three failed inside the Iggy client before).

The iggy consumer now sets the payload as a byte[] body. Previously it was a String decoded with the default charset of the JVM, which corrupted binary payloads. Camel converts the byte[] to a String where a String is needed, with the charset of the exchange (CamelCharsetName) if set, but code that reads the body with getBody() and expects a String must now use getBody(String.class) or add convertBodyTo(String.class) to the route.

camel-jira - the newIssues consumer reads every new issue

A poll of the newIssues consumer read only the newest maxResults (default 50) of the issues created since the previous poll, and the older ones were never consumed. A poll now reads every page of the search and can route more than maxResults issues; maxResults is the page size of that search (at most 50). There is no upper bound, so the first poll after a long outage or a large import can send many search requests and route many issues in one batch.

On Jira Cloud the Jira consumers now read the pages of a search with the next page token, as Jira Cloud cannot page by start index: with maxResults above 50, a search with more than 50 results failed on the second page with UnsupportedOperationException.

camel-langchain4j-ingest - a body that is not text fails

In the default text modality, the langchain4j-ingest producer now ingests the text of a LangChain4j TextSegment or Document body, and fails the exchange with an InvalidPayloadException for a body that is not text, bytes, a stream, a Reader or a file, such as a Map or a POJO. Before, such a body was ingested as its toString(), for example TextSegment { text = …​ }. Convert the body to text before the endpoint.

camel-netty-http - an ambiguous request path is rejected when a security constraint is configured

A netty-http consumer configured with a securityConstraint now rejects, with HTTP 400, a request whose path contains . or .. segments (including their percent-encoded %2e form). Such a path is ambiguous: the security constraint, the consumer dispatch and the route may each interpret it differently, so with matchOnUriPrefix=true and a specific inclusion such as /admin/*, a request like /foo/../admin/x (or its encoded /foo/%2e%2e/admin/x form) could be matched against the inclusions and exclusions in a different form than the one the route handles. Rejecting it up front keeps the constraint decision and the routed path consistent. A well-behaved HTTP client normalizes dot-segments before sending, so normal traffic is unaffected.

For the same reason, such a consumer also rejects with HTTP 400 a request whose path contains an empty segment (//), a ; path parameter (also as %3b) or a \ (also as %5c). A backend the route forwards the request to may collapse the empty segment, strip the path parameter or treat the backslash as a /, and so act on a different path than the one the constraint was checked against. A route that needs matrix parameters (;) must not configure a securityConstraint on the same consumer.

This applies only when a securityConstraint is configured; a consumer without one is unchanged, and continues to receive the request path as sent.

camel-hashicorp-vault - properties without engine use the secret engine

A hashicorp: property without an engine, such as {{hashicorp:mysecret}} or {{hashicorp:mysecret#field}}, now reads the secret from the secret engine. Before, it read from the engine of the hashicorp: property resolved before it (or from a null engine), and {{hashicorp:mysecret#field}} always resolved to no value. Properties with an engine, such as {{hashicorp:secret:mysecret#field}}, are not affected.

A hashicorp: property without an engine and with a version, such as {{hashicorp:mysecret@2}}, now reads version 2 of the secret mysecret in the secret engine. Before, the @2 was kept as part of the secret name.

A hashicorp: property with a field of a secret that does not exist, such as {{hashicorp:secret:missing#field}}, now resolves to no value, as it already did without a field, so its default value or an optional placeholder ({{?hashicorp:secret:missing#field}}) applies. Before, without a default value, it failed with a NullPointerException.

camel-jetty, camel-servlet - mapHttpMessageBody=false leaves the message body null

With mapHttpMessageBody=false on a jetty or servlet consumer, the HTTP request body is not read and the message body is null, also when the route accesses it. Before, the body was not read up front, but it was still read from the request when the route first accessed it. As before, no attachments, Java serialized objects or form fields are mapped.

A route that needs the request body must keep mapHttpMessageBody=true (the default). A route that sets no reply body now replies with an empty body, instead of with the request body.

camel-aws2-ddb - aws2-ddbstream reads every shard to its end

The aws2-ddbstream consumer now reads a closed shard to its end before it reads its child shards, and uses streamIteratorType only for its start position. Before, records left in a closed shard could be skipped, and after a shard rotation the consumer started again from streamIteratorType: with FROM_START it routed records of the stream again, and with FROM_LATEST it skipped records already written to the new shards.

camel-beanio - BeanIOSplitter decodes files and streams with the charset of the exchange or its encoding

BeanIOSplitter read a file body with the default charset of the JVM, and a stream or byte array body with the charset of the exchange or the default charset of the JVM; its encoding option was not used. A file consumed with charset=ISO-8859-1, or a splitter with setEncoding(StandardCharsets.ISO_8859_1), had every non-ASCII character replaced. The splitter now decodes a file, stream or byte array body with the charset of the exchange (the CamelCharsetName header or exchange property, as set by the charset option of the file consumer), else with its encoding (default: the default charset of the JVM).
