When a body would be sent as an ObjectMessage and objectMessageEnabled is false, the error now names the class of the body. It also says how to send the body as text: convert it to a String first, or marshal it (for example to JSON). This applies to the components that build on camel-jms (camel-amqp, camel-activemq, camel-activemq6) and to camel-sjms2.

camel-amqp - the producers share one connection when the component creates the connection factory

When the amqp component creates the connection factory, from brokerUrl, from the host, port, username, password and useSsl options, or with the AMQPComponent.amqpComponent factory methods, its producers now send over one shared connection, which the component closes when it stops. Before, every message sent opened and closed a connection to the broker. This also applies to request/reply and to the polling consumer (for example pollEnrich). The consumers keep their own connections, and a message sent in a transacted route takes part in the transaction as before. As the messages go over one connection, they all go to the same broker when the URL leads to several brokers, for example behind a load balancer.

A connectionFactory set on the component or on an endpoint is used as before, and so is the connection factory of an endpoint with its own username and password. To open a connection for every message sent, as before, set the new cacheConnection option to false (camel.component.amqp.cache-connection = false).

camel-pulsar - the producer continues the sequence ids of the broker

The pulsar producer no longer passes the default initialSequenceId=-1 to the Pulsar client, so the client continues after the last sequence id the broker has for the producerName. Before, every start began again at sequence id 0, and with deduplication enabled on the broker the messages sent after a restart were dropped as duplicates (the send succeeded) until the sequence ids passed the ones of the previous run. A configured initialSequenceId of 0 or more is still used as before.

camel-pulsar - the deadLetterTopic option applies in retry mode

With enableRetry=true, the consumer did not pass deadLetterTopic to the Pulsar client, so a message that failed maxRedeliverCount times was sent to the client’s default dead letter topic (<topic>-<subscription>-DLQ, or <subscription>-DLQ when that topic already exists) instead of the configured one. It is now sent to deadLetterTopic. Messages already in the default topic stay there.

With enableRetry=true and no maxRedeliverCount, the consumer failed to start with a NullPointerException. It now starts without a dead letter policy, as the maxRedeliverCount option documents, and the Pulsar client applies its own in retry mode: 16 redeliveries and its default retry letter and dead letter topics. As without retry mode, retryLetterTopic and deadLetterTopic only apply when maxRedeliverCount is set. The consumer logs a WARN when it starts with one of these topics but no maxRedeliverCount (and for retryLetterTopic without enableRetry).

camel-twitter - twitter-search applies its query options again

Since Camel 4.0 (the upgrade to Twitter4J 4.1, whose Query is immutable) the twitter-search endpoint sent only the keywords. The consumer ignored filterOld (default true), lang, count and the geolocation options, so it routed the same tweets again at every poll. The producer ignored sinceId, lang, count and the CamelTwitterSinceId, CamelTwitterMaxId, CamelTwitterSearchLanguage and CamelTwitterCount headers. The options are applied again: a poll returns only the tweets newer than the last one consumed, and a page has count tweets (default 5; Twitter’s own default of 15 applied before). Raise count and numberOfPages if a search can match more tweets than that between two polls. A consumer with type=direct returned nothing when keywords were given, and now runs the search at startup.

camel-kudu - a row error of Kudu fails the exchange

The insert, delete, update and upsert operations of the producer ignored the answer of Kudu. The Kudu client does not throw for a row that Kudu rejected; it returns a row error, for example for an insert of a key that is already present, or for an update or a delete of a row that is not found. Such an exchange completed as if the row had been written. A write that timed out or could not reach a tablet server was reported the same way, and completed the exchange too; for such a write it is not known whether the row was written. The producer now fails the exchange in both cases with a KuduRowErrorException (getRowError() returns the row error of the Kudu client, whose status tells the kind of error). Routes that relied on rejected rows being skipped silently can use the upsert operation, or handle the exception, for example with onException(KuduRowErrorException.class).handled(true).

The producer also closes the Kudu session of each operation. The sessions stayed registered with the Kudu client until the client was closed, one more for every exchange.

camel-ldif - the connection is bound for every message, and the status of a failed change

The producer closes the LdapConnection after each message. A connection bean that is not a prototype then reconnected without a bind for the next message, so its changes failed with modifications require authentication. The producer now binds such a connection again, with the name and credentials of its LdapConnectionConfig. A route that called bind() on the connection before each message can drop that step.

The status of a failed change is now the LDAP result code, the DN of the entry and the message from the server, such as entryAlreadyExists (68) on uid=test1,ou=test,dc=example,dc=org, instead of only the message (which OpenLDAP leaves empty for some failures). A successful change is still success.

An empty or null body now gives an empty String as the result, instead of null. A missing connection bean fails with a NoSuchBeanException instead of a NullPointerException.

camel-mllp - the producer verifies the host name of the server over TLS

When sslContextParameters is set (on the endpoint, or with useGlobalSslContextParameters on the component), the MLLP producer now checks that the certificate of the server matches the host it connects to, with the same rules as an HTTPS client. Before, it accepted any certificate trusted by the trust store, whatever host the certificate was issued for.

A producer that connects with a host name or an IP address that the certificate does not list now fails the TLS handshake; an IP address must be listed as a subject alternative name of the certificate, and a producer URI without a host name (such as mllp://8888) cannot pass the check. Use a certificate that matches the host of the endpoint, or set the new hostnameVerification option to false to keep the previous behaviour.

camel-mybatis - the body is kept with InOut, and an Insert, Update or Delete without a body fails when the statement has parameters

With an InOut exchange, the Insert, InsertList, Update, UpdateList, Delete and DeleteList statement types now keep the message body, as they already did with InOnly. Before, the body was null after the call unless the outputHeader option was set. The result is still in the CamelMyBatisResult header (or the outputHeader).

An Insert, Update or Delete with a null body (or a missing inputHeader) now fails with an IllegalArgumentException when the statement has parameters. Before, MyBatis ran the statement with NULL for every parameter: an Insert stored a row of nulls, and an Update without a WHERE set columns to null in every row. A statement without parameters still runs with a null body.

camel-aws2-s3 - streaming upload with the random or timestamp naming strategy names the first file too

With streamingUploadMode=true and namingStrategy=random (or timestamp), the first file uploaded by a producer was named after keyName alone (for example camel.txt), and only the later files got a UUID (or a timestamp) after keyName. A producer that was started again (a restart of the route or of the application), or another producer with the same keyName, wrote its first file to the same key and replaced the first file written before. Every file now gets the UUID (or the timestamp), the first one too: camel-<uuid>.txt. The progressive naming strategy is unchanged.

camel-aws2-s3 - streaming upload writes concurrent exchanges one at a time

With streamingUploadMode=true (without timestampGroupingEnabled), the producer now writes one exchange at a time, so exchanges sent concurrently (concurrent consumers, parallel splits, threads()) no longer lose objects or parts. The body of an exchange is read while the other exchanges of the same producer wait, and bodies that complete an upload of their own (batchSize or more, or batchMessageNumber=1) are no longer uploaded in parallel. To upload large bodies in parallel, use the producer without streamingUploadMode.

camel-atom - sortEntries sorts by the updated date and routes the oldest entry first

With sortEntries=true the atom consumer sorted the entries of a feed by their published date and routed them from the newest to the oldest one. With the default idempotent strategy (which skips an entry that is not newer than the one routed before it) only some entries of a feed were routed, and the others were skipped.

This is fixed. The entries are now sorted by their updated date (by the published date for an entry without an updated date), which is the date the idempotent strategy compares, and they are routed from the oldest to the newest one, as camel-rss does and as Camel 3.x did. Entries are no longer skipped because of the routing order, also when an older entry was updated after newer ones were published.

Routes using sortEntries=true with idempotent=false get the entries in this order too: the entry that was updated first is routed first, where the entry that was published last was routed first before.

camel-opensearch - the credentials are only sent to the nodes of the cluster

The user and password are now offered only to the nodes the client talks to: the hosts of hostAddresses and, when enableSniffer is set, the nodes the sniffer finds, matching the scheme, host and port. Every node of a multi-host cluster still receives them. Previously they were registered for any host. A request to another host, including the host of a node with another scheme or port, is now sent without them. A client set with the client or openSearchClient option is not changed.

camel-aws-cloudtrail - the consumer looks again for events that CloudTrail shows late

The consumer polls from the newest event time it has seen, so an event that CloudTrail made visible after a newer one was never routed. Now, every lookBackInterval (default 1 minute), the consumer also looks up the events from lookBackWindow (default 10 minutes) before now up to that newest event time, and routes those it has not routed yet. The normal polls send the same LookupEvents requests as before. The late-event lookup adds ceil(N / maxResults) requests per lookBackInterval, at most one per poll, where N is the number of events in that range: with the defaults one request per minute up to 50 events in 10 minutes, 20 per minute for 1000 events, and none once no event was seen for 10 minutes. AWS allows two LookupEvents requests per second per account and Region. Set lookBackWindow=0 to disable the late-event lookup.

camel-aws2-s3 - a listed object that cannot be fetched is skipped

When the consumer lists the bucket, a failure to get one of the listed objects (or to download its content) no longer fails the poll, as long as another object of that listing could be got: the object is skipped, the other listed objects are consumed in that poll, and the skipped object is tried again by a later poll. An object that no longer exists (for example deleted by another consumer after the listing) is skipped with a log line at DEBUG. Any other failure is given to the exception handler of the consumer, with the key in the message: by default that is a WARN with the stack trace, and with bridgeErrorHandler=true the route’s error handler gets it. This happens at every poll that lists an object that can never be read (for example archived, or access denied), until it can be read or is removed from the bucket. Such a poll is a successful poll, so it does not count for backoffErrorThreshold.

The poll still fails, as before, when none of the listed objects could be got and at least one get failed with an error other than that the object no longer exists (for example expired credentials, no permission to get objects, throttling): the failure of the first object is then the failure of the poll, and backoffErrorThreshold applies. In both cases the objects are no longer kept in the inProgressRepository; before, the object (or a new object with the same key) was skipped by all later polls until the endpoint was restarted. With the fileName option, a failed get still fails the poll.

camel-aws2-s3 - the producer stores the whole body when the content-length header is stale

When the length of the body is known without reading it (a byte[], String, file, Path or cached stream), the producer now uploads it with that length, and the CamelAwsS3ContentLength header is only used for a stream whose length is not known. Previously the header took precedence: after a route changed the body of an object read by the aws2-s3 consumer (which sets the header), a longer body was stored cut to the old length and a shorter one failed.

camel-aws2-s3 - the consumer gets each object right before routing it, and ignoreBody no longer downloads objects

The consumer now gets each listed object right before its exchange is routed. Before, it got every object of the poll before it routed the first one: with the default includeBody=true the content of every object of the poll was in memory at the same time, and with includeBody=false the streams of all the objects of the poll were open at the same time. A poll of more than 50 objects (maxMessagesPerPoll) with includeBody=false then ran out of the 50 connections that the HTTP client of the AWS SDK has by default, and the AWS SDK aborted the streams that were not read within 60 seconds, so most of the exchanges of the poll failed.

This changes the following:

  • The batch properties count the listed objects of the poll. An object that is skipped when it is got (it was deleted after the listing; it cannot be got; or it is a folder and includeFolders is false) is still counted: CamelBatchSize can be higher than the number of exchanges routed for the poll, CamelBatchIndex has a gap, and when the skipped object is the last listed one, no exchange of the poll has CamelBatchComplete set to true. Before, they counted only the exchanges that were routed. The number of messages of the poll (used by greedy and sendEmptyMessageWhenIdle) still counts the exchanges routed. An aggregator with completionFromBatchConsumer then does not complete the group of a poll in which an object is skipped: the group waits for the exchanges of the next poll. This happens with includeFolders=false and folders in the bucket, or with several consumers of the same bucket (such as several pods of the same application). Such a route can use completionTimeout instead of completionFromBatchConsumer (not both), with a timeout longer than the time between two exchanges of a poll, and shorter than the delay of the consumer minus the completionTimeoutCheckerInterval (1 second by default).

  • An object that cannot be got is given to the exception handler of the consumer once the other exchanges of the poll were routed, instead of before the first one.

  • A consumer that stops during a poll no longer gets the objects that it does not route.

  • The protected method AWS2S3Consumer.createExchanges(List<S3Object>) is removed.

With ignoreBody=true, the consumer, and the getObject operation of the producer, now send a HEAD request (HeadObject) instead of a GET request, so the content of the object is not downloaded. Before, the whole object was transferred and discarded. The headers are the same, and HeadObject needs the same s3:GetObject permission. However:

  • An object whose content cannot be got but whose metadata can is now consumed (or returned by getObject) instead of failing, and with deleteAfterRead (the default) deleted. For example an object archived in S3 Glacier that is not restored, or an object encrypted with SSE-KMS whose KMS key the role cannot use to decrypt (a GET needs kms:Decrypt, a HEAD does not).

  • The response to a failed HEAD request has no body: the S3Exception has the HTTP status code, but no S3 error code, except NoSuchKey for an object that does not exist.

  • The consumer no longer calls includeS3Object(ResponseInputStream) or createExchange(ResponseInputStream, String) for these objects, as there is no GET response: a subclass of AWS2S3Consumer that overrides them is not called with ignoreBody=true.

The getObject operation of the producer now also sets the CamelAwsS3LastModified and CamelMessageTimestamp headers, with or without ignoreBody, as the consumer does. A CamelMessageTimestamp header that the message had before the operation is replaced.

camel-minio - the producer stores the whole body when the content-length header is stale

When the length of the body is known without reading it (a file, byte[], String, Path or cached stream), the producer now uploads it with that length, and the CamelMinioContentLength header is only used for a stream whose length is not known. Previously the header took precedence, also over the length of a java.io.File body: after a route changed the body of an object read by the minio consumer (which sets the header), a longer body was stored cut to the old length and a shorter one failed.

camel-mina - the host name of the server is verified over SSL

When sslContextParameters is set (on the endpoint, or with useGlobalSslContextParameters on the component), the producer, and the consumer with clientMode=true, now check that the certificate of the server matches the host of the endpoint, with the same rules as an HTTPS client. Before, any certificate trusted by the trust store was accepted, whatever host the certificate was issued for.

A connection to a host name or an IP address that the certificate does not list now fails the SSL handshake; an IP address must be listed as a subject alternative name of the certificate. Use a certificate that matches the host of the endpoint, or set the new hostnameVerification option to false to keep the previous behaviour.

MINA does not pass a failed handshake on as an error: the producer fails with a timeout (after writeTimeout, or timeout while waiting for a reply), and the handshake error is logged at ERROR level by org.apache.mina.filter.ssl.SslHandler. A producer that starts timing out after the upgrade should be checked for a certificate that does not list the host.

camel-mina - the producer closes its session when a request times out

A synchronous mina producer (sync=true) now always closes its session when a request times out while waiting for a reply. Because a synchronous producer is pooled and reused for later exchanges, a session kept open after a timeout could receive the late reply to the timed-out exchange and hand it to the next exchange that reused the same producer. The producer now discards that session, so the next exchange opens a fresh one.

The default behaviour is unchanged: disconnectOnNoReply defaults to true, which already closed the session on a timeout. Setting disconnectOnNoReply=false no longer keeps the producer session open after a timeout. The option now has no effect: the consumer never used it, and always closes the session when it has no reply to send.

camel-ftp - the host name of the FTPS server is verified

The ftps endpoint now checks, on the control connection, that the certificate of the server matches the host of the endpoint, with the same rules as an HTTPS client, with sslContextParameters as well as with the ftpClient.trustStore.* options. Before, any certificate accepted by the trust manager was accepted, whatever host the certificate was issued for. Data connections are not checked by host name.

A connection to a host name or an IP address that the certificate does not list now fails the TLS handshake, before login; an IP address must be listed as a subject alternative name of the certificate. Use a certificate that matches the host of the endpoint, or set the new hostnameVerification option to false to keep the previous behaviour. A custom ftpClient set on the endpoint is not changed.

camel-ftp, camel-sftp, camel-ftps, camel-mina-sftp, camel-smb - ${file:name} in moveExisting is the name of the file

With fileExist=Move, the ${file:name} token of moveExisting is now the name of the existing file in its directory, as the producer of ftp, ftps and sftp already used it with the default stepwise=true. So with stepwise=false, with a tempFileName, in mina-sftp and in smb, a relative moveExisting built from ${file:name}, ${file:onlyname} or literal text now gives the same result as with stepwise=true. Before, ${file:name} was the path of the file from the login directory (or the share), and since a relative moveExisting is put below the directory of the file, moveExisting=backup/${file:name} for hello.txt in the directory inbox/orders moved the existing file to inbox/orders/backup/inbox/orders/hello.txt; it is now moved to inbox/orders/backup/hello.txt. The tokens ${file:name.noext} and ${file:name.noext.single} read the same name and change the same way: inbox/orders/hello is now hello. A backup kept in the nested directory is no longer found there, for example by the check for an existing backup when eagerDeleteTargetFile=false, or by a job that reads the backups. ${file:parent}, ${file:onlyname} and ${file:ext} are unchanged, and so is a moveExisting without ${…​} such as moveExisting=backup.

In the same cases, a relative result is now left as it is only when it starts with the directory of the file as whole path segments (inbox/orders/backup/hello.txt); any other relative result is put below that directory. Before, a result that only started with the same characters was left as it was. So for a file in inbox/orders, moveExisting=${file:parent}-old/${file:onlyname} and moveExisting=inbox/orders-old/${file:onlyname} now move it to inbox/orders/inbox/orders-old/hello.txt instead of inbox/orders-old/hello.txt; to keep a directory next to the directory of the file, use a path relative to it, such as moveExisting=../orders-old/${file:onlyname} (camel-smb resolves the .. itself, as SMB servers do not accept it in a path). Such a path can lead out of the directory of the endpoint: jailStartingDirectory does not apply to moveExisting. The file component is not changed: there ${file:name} is still the path of the file, and the check is as before.

camel-aws-secrets-manager - the CloudTrail refresh task reads every event since the last check

With camel.vault.aws.refreshEnabled=true and without SQS notification, each check now reads every page of the PutSecretValue and UpdateSecret events since the previous check, including the events in the same second as the newest event seen before, so an update is no longer missed when more events than one page happened in between. The first check looks for the updates made after the task started: an update made before the start no longer reloads the context. A check sends two LookupEvents requests, one per event name, and one more per further 50 updates (before: always one request, for all Secrets Manager events); reads of secrets no longer add requests. LookupEvents allows two requests per second per account and Region.

Each check looks up these events from 10 minutes before the newest event seen (but not before the task started), and skips the events seen by an earlier check: CloudTrail can make an event visible after a newer one, and such an update was never read. The number of requests is as above, counted over that range.

camel-aws2-s3 - a timestamp window uploaded more than once is stored in more than one object

With streamingUploadMode=true and timestampGroupingEnabled=true, the upload of a time window is completed when it reaches batchMessageNumber messages (10 by default) or batchSize bytes, or when streamingUploadTimeout runs. A later message of the same window started a new upload with the same key, and S3 replaced the object of the first upload with it, so the messages of the earlier uploads were lost (unless the bucket is versioned). A later upload of a window now gets the number of the uploads of that window before it after the time range: for example grouped-messages_20240101_0800_0800-0805.txt, then grouped-messages_20240101_0800_0800-0805-1.txt, grouped-messages_20240101_0800_0800-0805-2.txt, and so on. The first upload of a window keeps the name it had. The first time a producer uploads a window, it lists the objects of the window in the bucket (one ListObjectsV2 request per window), so after a restart it continues after the highest number stored; this needs the s3:ListBucket permission, and without it the producer logs a WARN and numbers the uploads of the window from the first one. These keys do not sort in upload order (-1 sorts before the first upload’s name, -10 before -2). Producers running at the same time with the same keyName still need distinct key names.

camel-aws2-kinesis - the consumer continues after the last record when a shard iterator expired

When a cached shard iterator has expired (an iterator is valid for 5 minutes, so after a route was suspended for longer, or with a delay or back-off above 5 minutes), the consumer now continues after the last record it routed from that shard. Previously it requested a new iterator of the configured iteratorType: with TRIM_HORIZON (the default) it routed all the records of the shard again, with LATEST it skipped the records stored meanwhile.

A shard created by a split or a merge while the consumer is running is now read from its first record, whatever the iteratorType. Previously, with LATEST, the records stored in the new shard before the consumer listed it were skipped. The shards that exist when the consumer starts are read from the configured iteratorType, as before.

camel-aws2-kinesis - the consumer fetches up to 1000 records per shard in each poll

The default of maxResultsPerRequest is now 1000 (it was 1). In each poll the consumer asks each shard for up to that many records, so with the default delay of 500 ms it can read up to about 2000 records per second from a shard (within the Kinesis quota of 2 MiB per second), instead of about 2. A poll now routes more records, and holds up to 1000 records per shard in memory while they are routed. The KCL consumer (useKclConsumers=true) does not use this option.

A graceful shutdown waits until the consumer has routed all the records of the poll that is running. With 1000 records per shard and a route that takes 50 ms per record, a poll takes about 50 seconds, which is more than the default shutdown timeout of 45 seconds. The shutdown is then forced, and the records of the poll that are not routed yet fail with a RouteStoppingException. Before, a poll held one record per shard. Lower maxResultsPerRequest, or raise the shutdown timeout, so that the records of a poll are routed within the timeout.

To keep the previous behaviour, set maxResultsPerRequest=1. See the AWS Kinesis component for how maxResultsPerRequest, delay and greedy relate to the Kinesis read quotas of a shard.

camel-graphql - the credentials are only sent to the authority of httpUri

The accessToken (with its jwtAuthorizationType) is now added to a request only when the request goes to the scheme, host and port of httpUri, and the username and password are registered for that authority only. Previously neither was bound to the authority of httpUri. A request to another authority, including the same host with another scheme or port, is now sent without them. A route that needs to authenticate to another authority must address it in httpUri. When proxyHost is set, the username and password are no longer offered to the proxy when it asks for authentication.

The producer also no longer takes the request URL from the CamelHttpUri, CamelHttpPath, CamelHttpQuery and other camel-http control headers of the message. It did so since Camel 4.19, when the component moved onto camel-http; the request now always goes to httpUri, as it did before. For the same reason the CamelHttpMethod and CamelHttpProtocolVersion headers no longer apply, and the request is always a POST.

camel-elasticsearch - the credentials are only sent to the nodes of the cluster

The user and password are now offered only to the nodes the client talks to: the hosts of hostAddresses and, when enableSniffer is set, the nodes the sniffer finds, matching the scheme, host and port. Previously they were registered for any host. A request to another host, including the host of a node with another scheme or port, is now sent without them. A client set with the client option is not changed.

camel-elasticsearch, camel-opensearch - the client set on the component is not closed by Camel

A RestClient set with the client option of the component, or autowired from the registry, is shared by all the endpoints of the component. A producer that stopped (its route stopped or removed, or Camel stopped), and with disconnect=true every request, closed it, and from then on every endpoint of the component failed its requests until the application was restarted. Camel now closes only the clients its producers create from hostAddresses. Close a client you set on the component yourself when the application no longer needs it (a client that is a Spring bean is closed by Spring).

camel-jetty - a multipart text field that occurs several times

A text field of a multipart/form-data request that occurs several times (for example a group of checkboxes) is now mapped to a header whose value is the list of all its values, as a repeated query or form-urlencoded parameter already is. Before, the header had only the first value and the others were lost. Jetty also returns the query string values of the same name, so a name that is in both the query string and a multipart text field is mapped to a list of all these values too, as it already is for a form-urlencoded request.

camel-servlet and camel-jetty - gzip request body that arrives after the headers

The servlet and Jetty consumers uncompressed a request body with Content-Encoding: gzip only when some of its bytes had already arrived when the consumer started to read it. When the client sent the body later than the headers (for example a client that sends Expect: 100-continue), the route got the compressed bytes. Such a body is now uncompressed like any other gzip request body. A route that uncompressed the body itself in that case must no longer do so. An empty body is passed on as before, and so is the body of an exchange with the CamelSkipGzipEncoding property. A request with Content-Encoding: gzip whose body is not gzip data now fails with a ZipException before the route is called (Jetty answers 500) also when the body arrives after the headers, where the route got the bytes as they were sent; it already failed in this way when the body had arrived. With disableStreamCache=true the consumer now waits for the first bytes (the GZIP header) of a gzip request body before it calls the route; with stream caching, the default, it already read the whole body before that. The same applies to every consumer that reads the request body with DefaultHttpBinding.parseBody of camel-http-common, such as the Spring Boot platform-http consumer with useStreaming=true.

camel-undertow - form fields are decoded as UTF-8

The consumer now decodes the fields of an application/x-www-form-urlencoded or multipart/form-data request that declares no charset as UTF-8, the encoding browsers use. Before, Undertow’s default ISO-8859-1 was used, so café became cafÃ© in the headers and the body map. A charset that the request or a part declares is still used: a client that sends ISO-8859-1 form data without declaring a charset now gets U+FFFD replacement characters instead of its text, and must declare its charset (for example application/x-www-form-urlencoded; charset=ISO-8859-1).

camel-milvus, camel-neo4j, camel-qdrant, camel-weaviate - the embeddings transformers without the embedding header, and the text of neo4j:rag

The milvus:embeddings, neo4j:embeddings, qdrant:embeddings and weaviate:embeddings data type transformers now fail with an IllegalArgumentException that names the CamelLangChain4jEmbeddingsVector header when the message does not have it, as pgvector:embeddings does. Before, they failed with a NullPointerException.

The neo4j:rag data type transformer now converts a text value that is not a String, such as a number, to a String, and gives an empty String for a null text, as for a row without text. Before, a value of another type failed with a ClassCastException, and a null text gave a null element.

camel-undertow - rest-dsl and plain consumers on the same port are both served

When rest-dsl consumers and plain undertow: consumers use the same port, both kinds are now served. Before, only the kind that registered first on the port was served, the other answered 404, and stopping the last consumer of one kind stopped the server for the other. A request now goes to the rest-dsl consumers first, and to the plain endpoints when no rest-dsl consumer matches it, so a plain catch-all (for example matchOnUriPrefix=true on /, for static files) and a REST API can share a port. When a rest-dsl path and a plain path both match a request, the rest-dsl consumer gets it, even when the plain path is more specific (for example a plain /users/me next to a rest-dsl /users/{id}): give the plain endpoint another path or port. A request under the static part of a plain endpoint path with a template (such as /api/x/y for /api/{name}) that no template matches now gets 404 (it got 405 before).

camel-undertow - the static route of a base path answers it next to a path template

When one route consumes a path template, such as /orders/{id}, and another route the static path of its base path (/orders, exact or with matchOnUriPrefix=true), a request for /orders itself (also /orders/, and / for templates of the root path) now goes to the static route. Before, the template route answered it with an empty id when it had started first, or when the static route used matchOnUriPrefix. When the static route restricts the HTTP methods (httpMethodRestrict), such a request with another method now gets 405 instead of reaching the template route. A matchOnUriPrefix route of the base path now also starts when the template route started first (it failed with Duplicate handlers on a path). An exact static route (without matchOnUriPrefix) now answers only its own path: a deeper path that no template matches, such as /orders/a/b, gets 404, as without the template route (before, the static route answered it, or it got 405 when the static route restricted the methods). To keep the old behaviour, where the template route answers /orders with an empty id, drop the static route of the base path: a template route alone still serves the base path.

camel-huaweicloud-obs - a listed object that cannot be got is skipped

When the consumer lists the bucket, a failure to get one of the listed objects (for example access denied, or an archived object that is not restored) no longer fails the poll, as long as another object of that listing could be got: the object is skipped, the other listed objects are consumed, and the skipped object is tried again by a later poll. The failure is given to the exception handler of the consumer, with the key in the message (by default a WARN with the stack trace, at every poll that lists the object). Before, the poll failed, so the other objects listed with it were not consumed either, and when the bucket fitted in one listing (maxMessagesPerPoll, default 10) the consumer consumed no object at all. The poll still fails when none of the listed objects could be got. With the fileName option, a failed get still fails the poll.

camel-huaweicloud-dms - createInstance fails on a body that is not the request

The createInstance operation of hwcloud-dms now reads the JSON of a CreateInstanceRequestBody from text, bytes, a stream or a file, such as the body of an HTTP request or the file of a file consumer, and from a Map with the same field names, such as the JSON unmarshalled by an earlier step. Before, only a String body was read: JSON given as bytes, a stream, a file or a Map was ignored without a warning, and the instance was created from the endpoint options and the exchange properties.

A body that is not a CreateInstanceRequestBody or a valid JSON representation of it now fails the exchange with an IllegalArgumentException. Before, such a body was ignored (a String with a WARN log) and the instance was created from the options. The instance is now created from the options only when the exchange has no body, or an empty one. A route that creates the instance from the options while it carries another body, such as a text from an earlier step, must clear the body first, for example with setBody and an empty constant.

updateInstance reads the JSON of an UpdateInstanceRequestBody from bytes, a stream, a file or a Map too. A body that is not a valid JSON of it now fails with an IllegalArgumentException, where it failed with the exception of Jackson.

camel-github2 - consumers and items of a poll that were not routed

The github2 consumers (commit, event, pullRequest, pullRequestComment, tag) remembered every item of a poll as consumed before routing it. Items that were not routed because the route was suspended during the poll were never routed. They are now routed by the first poll after the route is resumed.

The commit consumer also lost the new commits of a poll in which the GitHub lookup of an author or a committer failed (for example a 404 for a deleted account, or a server error). Such a commit is now routed, and a WARN is logged for it. Its CamelGitHubCommitAuthor and CamelGitHubCommitCommitter headers then have the name that git recorded in the commit instead of the name of the GitHub profile, and are not set when the commit has no name either.

camel-huaweicloud-functiongraph - the whole result of a function without a body field

The invokeFunction operation of hwcloud-functiongraph now gives the whole result of the function as the message body when the result is not a JSON object with a top-level body field. Before, the body was null when the result was a JSON object without a body field, and the call failed with a Gson JsonSyntaxException when the result was a JSON array, another JSON value or plain text. A result with a body field, such as the result of a function behind an API Gateway trigger, still gives that field, and no body when the field is null. A route that tested for a null body after the call must now test the result instead.

camel-slack - consumer and a suspended route

When a route is suspended (RouteController.suspendRoute, also from a route policy that suspends the route such as DurationRoutePolicy, or CamelContext.suspend()) while the slack consumer routes the messages of a poll, the consumer stops after the message that is being routed. The other messages of that poll were skipped when the route was resumed. They are now kept and routed by the first poll after the resume, together with the messages posted in the meantime. They are kept in memory only: a route that is stopped and started again does not route them.

camel-huaweicloud-iam - the JSON results use the field names of the IAM API

The operations of hwcloud-iam now write their results (the users or the groups of listUsers, listGroups and getGroupUsers, and the user or the group of getUser, updateUser and updateGroup) as JSON with the field names of the IAM API. These are the names that the JSON requests of updateUser and updateGroup already used, and that hwcloud-dms writes too. Before, the results used the Java field names of the Huawei Cloud SDK. For example, domainId is now domain_id, pwdStatus is pwd_status, lastProjectId is last_project_id, passwordExpiresAt is password_expires_at, createTime is create_time and isDomainOwner is is_domain_owner. A route that reads these fields, for example with the JSONPath $.domainId, must use the new names.

The component no longer depends on Gson.

camel-mail - mails of a poll that were not routed, with an idempotentRepository

With an idempotentRepository, the mail consumer now removes the keys of the mails that a poll retrieved but did not route (the consumer was stopped in the middle of the batch, or the poll failed on a later mail), so that a later poll routes them. Before, they were skipped as duplicates by every later poll. As a consequence, a mail that the consumer cannot read (with the default skipFailedMessage=false and handleFailedMessage=false) now fails every poll until it is removed, as it does without an idempotentRepository. Before, it failed only the first poll and was then skipped, together with the mails retrieved before it. Set skipFailedMessage or handleFailedMessage to skip such a mail.

camel-huaweicloud-smn - a Map, a List or a POJO is published as JSON, and the CamelHwCloudSmnMessageId property

The hwcloud-smn producer now publishes a Map, a List or a POJO in the message body as JSON. Before, it published the toString() of the body, such as {orderId=1, status=shipped} for a Map. A Map or a List that cannot be written as JSON now fails with an IllegalArgumentException. Text, bytes, a stream, a file, an XML Source or DOM node, and a single value such as a number are published as text, as before.

The id of the published message is now set in the CamelHwCloudSmnMessageId exchange property (SmnProperties.MESSAGE_ID). The CamelHwCloudSmnMesssageId exchange property (SmnProperties.SERVICE_MESSAGE_ID), whose name has a typo, is still set to the same id, but it is deprecated and will be removed in a future release: read CamelHwCloudSmnMessageId instead.

camel-smpp - the host name of the SMSC is verified over SSL

With smpps (or usingSSL=true), directly or through an HTTP proxy, the connection now checks that the certificate of the SMSC matches the host it connects to, with the same rules as an HTTPS client. Before, any certificate trusted by the JVM was accepted, whatever host it was issued for.

A connection to a host name or an IP address that the certificate does not list now fails the SSL handshake; an IP address must be listed as a subject alternative name of the certificate. Use a certificate that matches the host of the endpoint, or set the new hostnameVerification option to false to keep the previous behaviour.

A mismatch shows as the producer or consumer failing to start, with an IOException whose cause names the host that was checked:

java.io.IOException: No subject alternative names matching IP address 127.0.0.1 found
    Caused by: javax.net.ssl.SSLHandshakeException: No subject alternative names matching IP address 127.0.0.1 found

camel-netty-http - the request body read as an InputStream

The body of a request received by the consumer is a NettyChannelBufferStreamCache. Its read() method now returns a byte as a value from 0 to 255, and -1 at the end of the body, as java.io.InputStream requires. Before, a byte from 0x80 was returned as a negative value (0xFF as -1, which a reader takes for the end of the body), and the method threw an IndexOutOfBoundsException at the end. markSupported() now returns false (mark(int) never did anything): a reader that needs mark support has to wrap the stream, for example in a BufferedInputStream. reset() goes back to the start of the body, as before.

camel-netty-http - securityConfiguration is copied per endpoint

When securityConfiguration is set on the NettyHttpComponent, each endpoint now receives an independent copy of the configuration. Modifying securityConfiguration.* options on an endpoint URI no longer alters the component-level configuration or other endpoints.

camel-netty-http - the endpoint URI includes the endpoint options

The endpoint URI of a netty-http endpoint (Endpoint.getEndpointUri()) is now its full URI, with the component scheme and the endpoint options, as for netty and most other components. For example, the endpoint netty-http:http://localhost:8080/foo?throwExceptionOnFailure=false now has the endpoint URI netty-http://http://localhost:8080/foo?throwExceptionOnFailure=false. Before, it was http://localhost:8080/foo.

Endpoints whose URIs differ only in their options are therefore no longer equal, and each of them gets its own producer when a ProducerTemplate, toD or another EIP with a dynamic URI sends to them. Before, these endpoints used the producer of the endpoint that was used first, with the options of that endpoint.

The request is still sent to the same HTTP address, which NettyHttpEndpoint.getAddressUri() returns. The new endpoint URI shows in:

  • the name of an auto-mocked endpoint, for example mock:netty-http:http://localhost:8080/foo instead of mock:http:localhost:8080/foo. A mockEndpoints pattern such as http* no longer matches a netty-http endpoint, and netty-http* does.

  • the name of the endpoint in JMX, for example netty-http://http://0.0.0.0:8080/foo.

  • the CamelToEndpoint exchange property.

  • tracing: a netty-http endpoint now gets the span decorator of netty-http instead of the one of http or https, and the include and exclude patterns are matched against the new endpoint URI.

  • the endpointName tag of the Micrometer exchange event metrics.

camel-netty - hostnameVerification verifies the host instead of failing

hostnameVerification=true on a netty producer, or on a consumer with clientMode=true, now checks that the certificate of the server matches the host the endpoint connects to. Before, it made the TLS handshake fail for every certificate, matching or not, with CertificateException: Hostname or IP address is undefined - the engine was created without a peer host, so the JDK had no name to compare against.

A route that had the option set was therefore not connecting at all, and needs no change. A route that sets it while connecting to a host the certificate does not list - most commonly an IP address, which has to appear as a subject alternative name of its own - now fails the handshake with the mismatch as the reason. The default is unchanged: hostnameVerification is false, and the host is then not checked.

Enabling the option also makes the JDK send the host as an SNI server name, as camel-netty-http already did. The default path still creates the engine without a peer and sends no SNI.

On a consumer that acts as a server the option is now ignored instead of being passed to the engine. It never verified anything there - the JDK does not support host name verification on the server side of a handshake, and rejected the client certificate with Endpoint Identification Algorithm HTTPS is not supported on the server side - so a consumer that asks for client certificates and had the option set now completes the handshake instead of failing it.

camel-netty-http - hostnameVerification is ignored on a consumer

A netty-http consumer no longer passes hostnameVerification=true to its TLS engine, as netty consumers already do not (see above). The JDK does not support host name verification on the server side of a handshake: a client that presented a certificate failed the handshake with Endpoint Identification Algorithm HTTPS is not supported on the server side, and a client that presented none was not checked at all. A consumer with needClientAuth=true and the option set now accepts trusted client certificates instead of rejecting all of them. Consumers that share a server through nettySharedHttpServer never applied the option, and are unchanged. The option still verifies the host on a netty-http producer.

camel-alibaba-oss - a listed object that cannot be got is skipped

When the consumer lists the bucket, a failure to get one of the listed objects (for example access denied, or an archived object that is not restored) no longer fails the poll, as long as another object of that listing could be got: the object is skipped, the other listed objects are consumed, and the skipped object is tried again by a later poll. The failure is given to the exception handler of the consumer, with the key in the message (by default a WARN with the stack trace, at every poll that lists the object). Before, the poll failed and the next poll listed the same page again, so one such object stopped the consumer from consuming any object. The poll still fails when none of the listed objects could be got, and the next poll then reads the next page of the listing.

camel-alibaba-fc - invokeFunction sends a stream or a file as its bytes

The invokeFunction operation of alibaba-fc now sends a stream or a file body (an InputStream, a java.io.File, or a GenericFile from a file consumer) as its bytes, as they are. Before, it read such a body as text and sent that text in UTF-8, which changed binary data. A String is still sent in UTF-8, a byte[] as it is, and no body as an empty payload.

A body that does not convert to bytes, such as a Map or a POJO, is no longer sent as its toString(): it now fails with an InvalidPayloadException. Marshal such a body first, for example to JSON with marshal().json().

camel-dropbox - search and get read every page of the result

The search operation (producer and consumer) now returns every match that the service gives. Previously it returned only the first page of the matches (the Dropbox SDK asks for 100 matches per page), and the rest was dropped without any sign. A search with many matches now sends one request per page, for the consumer at each poll. When one of these requests fails, the operation fails and no match is returned, as before.

The get operation on a folder (producer and consumer) now downloads every file of the folder. Previously it downloaded only the files of the first page of the folder listing. As before, the content of all the files is in the message body, so a large folder needs more memory than it did when only the first page was read.

camel-alibaba-kms - encrypt encrypts a stream or a file as its bytes

The encrypt operation of alibaba-kms now encrypts a stream or a file body (an InputStream, a java.io.File, or a GenericFile from a file consumer) as its bytes, as they are. Before, it read such a body as text and encrypted that text in UTF-8, which changed binary data, so decrypt did not give the same bytes back. A String is still encrypted in UTF-8, a byte[] as it is, and the plaintext option or the CamelAlibabaKmsPlaintext header still win over the body.

A body that does not convert to bytes, such as a Map or a POJO, is no longer encrypted as its toString(): it now fails with an InvalidPayloadException. Marshal such a body first, for example to JSON with marshal().json().

camel-pqc - a configured KeyGenerator or KeyAgreement is used by one exchange at a time

When Camel creates the instance itself, the KEM operations of the pqc component (including the hybrid KEM operations) and the PQC data format now use a new KeyGenerator, and for the hybrid operations a new KeyAgreement, for every operation. A configured instance is still shared: a registry bean, which is autowired into every pqc endpoint, a KeyGenerator set on the data format, or the default material used when no key material is configured. Each operation now holds its lock from init until the result is returned, so concurrent exchanges that use it run one at a time.

A started PQC data format no longer keeps a KeyGenerator of its own: getKeyGenerator() returns only a configured instance and, unless one is configured, the CamelPQCKemAlgorithm header now selects the KEM algorithm of each operation.

camel-pqc - message bodies are signed and verified over their bytes

The sign, verify, hybridSign and hybridVerify operations now read a java.io.File or java.nio.file.Path body, and a file from a file consumer (a GenericFile body), as bytes from the file, the same way as a byte[] or InputStream body. A String (or any CharSequence) body is encoded as UTF-8, and any other body that has a binary type converter (for example a java.nio.ByteBuffer, a java.io.ByteArrayOutputStream, a Netty ByteBuf or a Vert.x Buffer) is signed over those bytes.

A body with no binary representation (for example a Map or a POJO) is no longer signed over its toString(): it is now rejected with an InvalidPayloadException. Convert such a body to a String, byte[] or other binary type earlier in the route.

Signatures created by earlier versions over a File, Path, GenericFile or other non-byte[]/InputStream body may not verify and need to be created again.

camel-alibaba-mns - binary bodies are kept, the message id and MD5 are headers, and receiveMessage is not an operation

The sendMessage and publishMessage operations of alibaba-mns now send a byte[], a stream or a file body (an InputStream, a java.io.File, or a GenericFile from a file consumer) as its bytes, as they are. Before, they read such a body as text and sent that text in UTF-8, which changed binary data, and a binary file from a file consumer failed to convert. A String is still sent as it is, and any other body, such as a Map, a List or a POJO, is still sent as its toString().

The consumer now gives the body of a message as a byte[] when its bytes are not UTF-8 text, such as the bytes of a binary file. Before, it gave a String in which such bytes were replaced. A message of UTF-8 text is still a String.

sendMessage and publishMessage now set the CamelAlibabaMnsMessageId and CamelAlibabaMnsMessageBodyMd5 headers, as the docs say, so after a send these headers hold the id and the MD5 of the message that was sent, not of a message consumed earlier in the route. They are still set as exchange properties too, as before.

receiveMessage is no longer a value of the operation option: the producer never supported it, and the consumer, which receives the messages of a queue, does not use the option. A producer with operation=receiveMessage still fails, now with a message that says so. Remove operation=receiveMessage from consumer endpoints, which tooling that validates endpoint options now reports.

camel-minio - a listed object that cannot be got is skipped

When the consumer lists the bucket, a failure to stat or get one of the listed objects (for example access denied, or an object encrypted with a customer key the consumer does not have) no longer fails the poll, as long as another listed object could be got: the object is skipped, the other listed objects are consumed, and the skipped object is tried again by a later poll. The failure is given to the exception handler of the consumer, with the object name in the message (by default a WARN with the stack trace, at every poll that lists the object). Before, the objects listed after it in the same poll were not consumed, so one such object could keep the consumer from consuming the rest of the bucket. The poll still fails when none of the listed objects could be got, and when objectName is set. When a poll lists maxMessagesPerPoll objects, the next poll now continues after the last listed object (before, after the last object that was consumed), so an object that cannot be got at the end of a page does not hold the listing back.

camel-minio - Server-side encryption options now apply to MinioProducer

The serverSideEncryption and serverSideEncryptionCustomerKey endpoint options now apply to producer operations: - Put operations apply the configured server-side encryption or customer-supplied key. - Copy operations apply the configured encryption to the destination object (and serverSideEncryptionCustomerKey to the source object when configured). - Get operations (getObject and getObjectRange) pass the customer key when configured. - Only one of serverSideEncryption or serverSideEncryptionCustomerKey can be configured on an endpoint; configuring both throws IllegalArgumentException on producer startup.

camel-box - getEnterpriseEvents returns the events of every chunk

The getEnterpriseEvents operation of the event-logs API now returns all the events between after and before. Previously it returned only the first chunk of the event log (at most 500 events), and the rest was dropped without any sign. A wide after/before window now returns every event in one list: that is one request per 500 events plus one that finds the end of the log, and more memory. Narrow the window when the event log is large.

camel-aws2-eventbridge - the consumer keeps one target for a queue given with queueUrl

A consumer that uses its own queue (queueUrl) added a target with a new random id to the EventBridge rule at every start, and removed it on stop. Two consumers that share the queue, or a restart after a consumer was not stopped cleanly, left two targets for the queue, and EventBridge put every event on the queue twice. The target now has an id derived from the queue ARN, and stays on the rule when the consumer stops, so the queue also collects the events of the rule while no consumer runs. Remove the target from the rule yourself when you no longer use the queue, and remove targets with ids camel-eb- plus 8 characters that an earlier version left for the queue. An auto-created queue is not affected. During a rolling upgrade from an earlier version, events can be put on the queue twice until the last instance on the earlier version stops.

camel-chunk - message data printed with a direct tag is rendered literally

Previously the chunk component bound the message body, headers and variables into the Chunk engine so that Chunk re-parsed those values as template source: a value that itself contained Chunk tags (such as a body of {$headers.name}) had those tags expanded instead of being printed verbatim.

Message data bound to the engine is now neutralised so that a direct {$tag} printing the body, a header, a variable, or an element of a nested map, list or array renders the value literally instead of evaluating the tags inside it. Tag-free data is unchanged and Chunk’s string filters (for example slice, get, join) keep their existing behaviour. Routes that relied on message data being evaluated as Chunk template code must move that markup into the template itself.

As part of this, a non-List Collection value (such as a Set) is now bound as a List, so a direct {$body} prints Chunk’s list placeholder instead of the collection’s toString(), and a filter such as |join operates on its elements.

This does not cover every path: Chunk re-parses any string it produces while rendering, so the output of a filter ({$body|trim}), a string property of a bean body ({$body.name}, {$exception.message}) and string items bound by a {% loop %} are still parsed as template code. Keep untrusted data to direct {$tag} output; do not pass untrusted values through Chunk filters, bean properties or loops. If untrusted data must go through one of those paths, remove the { and } characters from it in the route before the chunk endpoint (Chunk has no escape for tags inside data), for example with a processor that rewrites the body or header.

camel-azure-storage-blob - a listed blob that cannot be got is skipped

When the consumer lists the container, a failure to get one of the listed blobs (for example a blob in the archive tier, or access denied) no longer fails the poll, as long as another listed blob could be got: the blob is skipped, the other listed blobs are consumed, and the skipped blob is tried again by a later poll. The failure is given to the exception handler of the consumer, with the blob name in the message (by default a WARN with the stack trace, at every poll that lists the blob). Before, the poll failed, and as every poll lists the container again, one such blob stopped the consumer from consuming any blob. The poll still fails when none of the listed blobs could be got.

camel-azure-eventhubs - an event that completes when the checkpoint timer of its window has run is checkpointed

With checkpointBatchTimeout, the consumer now starts a new checkpoint window when the timer of the current window has run, and no longer when the wall clock passes the time the timer was scheduled for. Before, an event that completed in the millisecond in which the timer ran (or while the timer was taking the count) was counted in the window whose count had already been taken: it was checkpointed only with the next event, and when the partition stayed quiet it was delivered again after a restart.

The CamelAzureEventHubsCheckpointUpdatedBy header can now be timeout. An event that completes after the scheduled time of its window, but before the (late) timer has run, joins that window and gets timeout; its offset is checkpointed by that timer. Before, such an event always started a new window, so timeout was practically never set. Routes that read this header can now see timeout as well as size.

camel-azure-functions - invokeFunction fails on an HTTP error status, and sends the body as bytes

The invokeFunction operation now fails the exchange with an org.apache.camel.http.base.HttpOperationFailedException when the function answers with an HTTP status of 400 or above, as the HTTP components do. The exception holds the status, the response body and the response headers. Previously the exchange succeeded, with the error response as the body and the status in the CamelAzureFunctionsStatusCode header. Set the new throwExceptionOnFailure option to false to keep the previous behaviour.

The request body is now sent as bytes: a stream or a file is sent as its content, and bytes are sent as they are. Previously a body other than text or bytes was sent as its toString(), and bytes were decoded as UTF-8. Text, a number or a boolean is still sent as its text. Another body that does not convert to bytes, such as a Map or a POJO, now fails the exchange: marshal it to JSON first. A request with a body now always has Content-Type: application/json unless the CamelAzureFunctionsHttpHeaders header sets a Content-Type; previously it had no Content-Type when that header was not set.

camel-azure-servicebus - the dead-letter reason and description are bounded

When the consumer dead-letters a message after a failed route with enableDeadLettering=true, the reason and the error description it writes are now capped at 4096 characters, the limit the Azure SDKs apply to both fields. A value that was cut ends with …​. The description holds the stack trace, so a deep one is now truncated rather than sent in full and rejected by the broker.

A settlement request the broker rejects is now reported through the consumer’s exception handler.

camel-azure-key-vault - the body after deleteSecret, and a Map or a POJO given to createSecret

The deleteSecret operation now sets the DeletedSecret of the Azure SDK as the message body, with the recovery id and the dates of the deletion and of the scheduled purge. Before, the message body after deleteSecret was always null. A route that relied on an empty body after deleteSecret must now clear the body itself.

The createSecret operation now stores a Map, a List or a POJO body as JSON, such as {"username":"admin","password":"secret"}, with dates and times as ISO-8601 text. Before, it stored the text the type converter gives (usually toString()), such as {username=admin, password=secret}. A POJO that has its own type converter to String, or a JAXB object, is now stored as the JSON of its properties as well: to store the text of the converter, convert the body to a String first, for example with convertBodyTo(String.class). A Map or a List that Jackson cannot serialize now fails with an IllegalArgumentException. Text, bytes, a stream or a file is stored as its text, as before.

camel-nats - the JetStream pull consumer subscribes again when its ephemeral consumer was deleted

With jetstreamEnabled=true and a pull subscription (the default), the nats consumer kept fetching from a JetStream consumer that the server had deleted, and consumed nothing more until the route was restarted. It now subscribes again when the server no longer has its ephemeral consumer (no durableName, and no durable name in the consumerConfiguration), which the server deletes after about 5 seconds without a pull request, for example while the client is disconnected, or loses on a restart. The new consumer starts at the first message the route did not acknowledge, so the route can see again the messages after that one (with ackPolicy=None, after the last message fetched). On a stream with WorkQueue retention, and on a stream that was created again since the route subscribed, the new consumer uses the consumer configuration as it is. The consumer asks the server for its consumer only after a fetch that ended empty well before pullFetchTimeout, at most every 5 seconds. A durable consumer is not created again: when it is gone (deleted by an administrator, or after its inactiveThreshold), the consumer logs a WARN and keeps fetching, and the fetches work again when the durable is created again with the same name.

camel-azure-cosmosdb - a client set on the component or autowired is not closed by Camel

A CosmosAsyncClient set with the cosmosAsyncClient option, or autowired from the registry, is shared by all the azure-cosmosdb endpoints. An endpoint that stopped (for example because its route was removed or reloaded) closed it, and from then on the other endpoints, and the application, failed every request until the application was restarted. Camel now closes only the clients that its endpoints create. Close a client you set yourself when the application no longer needs it (a client that is a Spring bean is closed by Spring).

camel-azure-cosmosdb - the JSON of an item is parsed into a Map

The createItem, upsertItem and replaceItem operations now parse a message body that holds the JSON of the item (a String, a byte[], a stream or a file) into a Map before they write it. Before, a JSON String or a file was written as a JSON string and failed, and a stream could not be serialized. A byte[] body was sent as it is, and the message body after the operation was the written item as a byte[]: it is now a Map, as for a Map body. If the next steps need the JSON as bytes, marshal the body, for example with marshal().json().

A List body, or JSON that is not one object (such as a JSON array), now fails with an IllegalArgumentException before Cosmos DB is called; before, Cosmos DB rejected it with an error 400. These operations write one item: send each item in its own exchange, for example with the Split EIP.

camel-telegram - a failed send fails the exchange once

When Telegram answers a message with an error status (for example 400 for an unknown chat), or with a body that cannot be read, the producer now fails the exchange once and the route stops there. Previously the route first continued after the producer as if the message had been sent, and then the exchange failed as well.

The exception of the exchange is the one that process() of the producer threw before: a RuntimeException that wraps an ExecutionException, with the RuntimeCamelException (status and body of the answer) as its cause, or the IOException when the request got no answer. The producer still waits for the answer before it returns, so the order of the messages is the same. The steps after the producer now run on the calling thread, and no longer on a thread of the HTTP client.

camel-nats - the JetStream push consumer subscribes again when its ephemeral consumer was deleted

With jetstreamEnabled=true and pullSubscription=false, the consumer now creates its ephemeral JetStream consumer again after a reconnect when the server deleted it, starting at the first message the route did not acknowledge, so the route can see again the messages after that one. A durable consumer is not created again (a WARN is logged), and a consumer deleted while the connection stays up is not detected. See the NATS component page for the details.

camel-vertx-websocket - the client consumer makes one reconnect attempt at a time

When the WebSocket of a consumer with consumeAsClient=true is closed, the consumer now makes one reconnect attempt at a time: while an attempt waits for the server (up to the connect timeout), the next runs of reconnectInterval are skipped, and maxReconnectAttempts counts these attempts one after the other. Previously every interval started another attempt, and when the server answered late each of them opened a WebSocket, so that every message was routed once per WebSocket. A consumer that is stopped no longer reconnects.

camel-core - a route from a route template has the template’s resource as its source

A route created from a route template or a Kamelet now returns the resource with the route template from Route.getSourceResource() (it returned null). This is the resource its source location (Route.getSourceLocation()) was already in, so the debugger and message history consoles show the source code of these routes too.

A route created from a templatedRoute also remembers the resource that declares the templatedRoute (Route.getTemplatedRouteResource(), the TemplatedRouteSource JMX attribute, and templatedRouteSource in the route dev console), and the route template id is available from Route.getRouteTemplateId(), the RouteTemplateId JMX attribute, and routeTemplateId in the route dev console.

Code that took a null source resource to mean that a route was created from a route template should use Route.isCreatedByRouteTemplate() instead.

camel-aws2-ddb - aws2-ddbstream consumer and a suspended route

When a route is suspended (RouteController.suspendRoute, also when a route policy calls it, or CamelContext.suspend()) while the aws2-ddbstream consumer routes the records of a poll, the consumer stops after the record that is being routed. The other records of that poll (up to maxResultsPerRequest, default 100) were skipped when the route was resumed. They are now routed after the resume. A consumer that is suspended directly (consumer.suspend()) routes all the records of its poll and was not affected.

camel-jackson and camel-jackson3 - the bodies of the data type transformers

When the X-Content-Class exchange property is not set, the application-json data type transformer now writes a Map body as a JSON object and a List (or another Collection) body as a JSON array, and reads text, bytes or a stream (an InputStream, a Reader or the stream cache) as text: a JSON object or array is kept as it is, and any other text becomes a JSON string, so Hello becomes "Hello". This includes text that is a JSON number, boolean, null or quoted string: 42 becomes "42", true becomes "true", and "Hello" with its quotes becomes "\"Hello\"". These bodies failed before. With the X-Content-Class exchange property set, the transformer still writes only a body of that class, or JSON object or array text. A Map entry with a null or empty value is left out, as the transformer already did for the properties of a POJO.

Bytes and a stream are read as text with the charset of the exchange (the CamelCharsetName header or exchange property, else UTF-8), and the JSON is written in UTF-8. A payload that is not text, such as Avro binary or an image, therefore becomes a JSON string with replacement characters. Encode such a payload first, for example in Base64, or give it a content class: a byte[] body with the X-Content-Class exchange property set to byte[] is written as a Base64 JSON string.

The avro-x-struct data type transformer now reads a JSON object or array given as text as JSON, as protobuf-x-struct does. Before, it read the text as Avro binary, which failed or gave a wrong JsonNode.

The avro-x-java-object and protobuf-x-java-object data type transformers of camel-jackson-avro and camel-jackson-protobuf now read a JsonNode body into the class of the X-Content-Class exchange property, as the camel-jackson3 ones do. Before, they failed with an UnsupportedOperationException.

The application-x-java-object, avro-x-java-object and protobuf-x-java-object data type transformers now fail on a null body with a CamelExecutionException, whose cause is an InvalidPayloadException, instead of a NullPointerException, when the X-Content-Class exchange property is set.

The avro-binary, avro-x-struct, protobuf-binary and protobuf-x-struct data type transformers of camel-jackson3-avro and camel-jackson3-protobuf now wrap a JacksonException in a CamelExecutionException, as their camel-jackson-avro and camel-jackson-protobuf counterparts and the camel-jackson3 JSON transformers do. The JacksonException is the cause of the CamelExecutionException.

camel-core - the route structure leaves out onException clauses that never run

A route with noErrorHandler runs none of its onException clauses, and a global onException is copied into every route of its route builder or file. The route structure now leaves these clauses out of such routes: the route-structure dev console, camel cmd route-structure, and the route diagrams drawn from it. Before, they were listed among the steps of the route.

camel-core - simple == compares a number and a numeric String by their values

The == and != operators of the simple language compare a number with a String that holds a number by their values, as <, ⇐, > and >= (and in when the left value is a number) do since Camel 4.23. Before, when the two were written differently, the String was converted to the type of the number and compared with equals (which also compares the scale of a BigDecimal), or the two were compared as text. So ${header.amount} == '10' is now true for a BigDecimal of 10.00 (it was false, while ${header.amount} ⇐ '10' and >= '10' were true), and so are ${header.count} == '3.0' for an Integer of 3 and ${header.text} == 10 for a String of 10.00. Spaces around the number and an exponent (such as ' 10' and '1e1') are allowed, as in the in operator, and < and > compare such a String by its exact value too (it was compared as a double). The same applies to =~, to contains on a collection, and to the Java DSL and mock predicates such as header("amount").isEqualTo("10"). Two String values, and a Double or Float compared with a String, compare as before. Use equals to compare the two values as text.

camel-diagram - a route diagram draws the steps of a route

A route diagram drew the onException clauses of a route among its steps, though they are not steps: a global onException is copied into every route, and runs only while a failure is handled. A route diagram now draws only the steps of the route: camel cmd route-diagram, the route-diagram dev console and its web component, and the route diagrams dumped by DumpRoutesStrategy. Use --errors (?errors=true in the dev console, the errors attribute of the <camel-route-diagram> web component, errors of DumpRoutesStrategy and @EnableRouteDiagramDump, and dumpRouteDiagramErrors of @CamelMainTest) to draw them in a frame of their own below the steps, with where the failures of the route go. A highlighted path that steps into an onException clause draws the frame as well.

The <camel-topology-diagram> web component no longer draws the error paths of the route topology as calls between routes. With its new errors attribute it draws the routes reached only on error in a frame below the other routes.

camel-jbang - the default name of a run of a whole directory

camel run --source-dir, and camel run of a directory, without --name name the integration after the directory. A folder of a project layout (src, main, resources, java, camel or routes) gives way to the first parent with another name, and . is the working directory. Before, the name was the one of the first file, in the order that the file system lists them, which could be any file, such as a Java class that a route uses. --name, and camel.main.name in application.properties, still win.

camel-jbang - route-diagram, route-structure and route-topology read Java sources without compiling them

Given Java source files, camel cmd route-diagram, camel cmd route-structure and camel cmd route-topology (and the camel_render_route_diagram MCP tool) now read the routes with the Java DSL parser, as camel transform route and camel overview do, instead of compiling the sources and loading their routes in a short-lived Camel. A step the parser cannot work out, such as a lambda or a processor, is shown as a placeholder, and the parts it could not read are listed (with --json, as an unread array in the JSON). Use the new --compile option to compile the sources and load their routes as before.

camel-diagram - a route diagram image draws the steps as cards

The PNG image of a route diagram draws each step as a card: an icon for the kind of step, in a circle of the color of its kind, the kind in bold (such as setBody) and its detail below (such as groovy: body.items). Before, a step was a box filled with the color of its kind, with its code (setBody[groovy{body.items}]) in one line. This applies to the dark, light and transparent themes, in camel cmd route-diagram, camel get history --diagram, camel get error --diagram, the camel_render_route_diagram MCP tool, the route-diagram developer console (format=png) and the RouteDiagramDumper. A card is taller than a box, so the image of the same route is taller. The ASCII and Unicode diagrams, the topology diagram and the web component do not change.

A custom color spec sets the colors of the cards with card, cardBorder, cardText and cardDetail. The text color no longer applies to the steps of a route diagram (it still does to the topology diagram).

The RouteDiagramRenderer(nodeWidth, fontSizeScaled, nodeTextPadding, metrics) constructor is deprecated, as the text of a card starts after its icon and nodeTextPadding is not used. Use RouteDiagramRenderer(nodeWidth, fontSizeScaled, metrics) instead.

A program that lays out routes with RouteDiagramLayoutEngine and draws them with RouteDiagramRenderer must call engine.setCardStyle(true) before layoutRoute. Without it, the cards get the height and text of the old boxes.

camel cmd route-diagram of a running application whose message history recorded the body types (such as in the dev profile) shows them on the arrows, with longer arrows to hold them. Use --body-types=false for the diagram without them.

camel cmd route-diagram of source files, and the camel_render_route_diagram MCP tool, check the routes as camel validate does, and mark the steps with a problem: a yellow border and a callout beside the route (a numbered list below a route with branches). An image of routes with problems is wider or taller. Use --problems=false for the diagram without them.

camel cmd route-diagram --output=diagram.png without --theme now saves the PNG image in the light theme. Before, the default theme was unicode also for a file, so the command saved the text diagram in diagram.txt instead. A diagram in the terminal is still text (unicode) by default, and a file that does not end with .png still gets the text diagram. Use --theme=unicode to save the text diagram.

The camel_render_route_diagram MCP tool also draws in the light theme when no theme is given (before, it failed without a theme).

camel-jbang - export to Quarkus keeps the names in quarkus.* keys
